### To the Eager Reader.

Behold, dear reader, take these writings set forth
With pious zeal by Weigel <note>Valentin Weigel (1533–1588)</note>, that great-souled herald;
Read them through; the value of judgment will be evident in all things:
May the third holy school <note>original: "Tertia schola sancta." This refers to the "School of Pentecost," a state of direct spiritual revelation which Weigel distinguished from the first school (nature) and the second school (the written word or reason)</note> guide you, once you have been won over.

->P. W. <note>Possibly the initials of the editor or a relative, such as Weigel's son Petrus</note><-

# Preface to the Favorable Reader.

I am compelled / favorable reader / by my
adversaries to describe my theology <note>original: "Theologiam"</note>, that is /
my public confession of
faith / and I call it the *Theology of Weigel* <note>original: "Theologiam VVeigelij"</note>, that is /
my theology,
not because it is mine alone and belongs to no other:
rather, it is mine and that of all the prophets and
apostles / also of all believers / from the beginning of the
world until the end / as such is contained in the books of the Old
and New Testament <note>original: "A. vnd N. T." (Alten und Neuen Testaments)</note> / It is the common gift.
I call it the *Theology of Weigel* (as mentioned)
because I profess it / as I wish to
know, teach, or believe nothing
else / than solely what is grounded in the writings of the
prophets and apostles. And although
all other sects likewise appeal to it and
refer to it just as I do; yet through this *Theology of Weigel*
it shall be made known and manifest / that I am no sectarian <term>Sectarius</term> <note>A term for a member of a faction or "sect" that breaks away from the established church; Weigel is defending himself against charges of starting his own cult</note>
nor a heretic nor a deceiver / therefore,
this should not be construed or interpreted as
arrogance or pride <note>original: "Arrogantz oder Hoffart"</note> / as if I
wished to hold a special theology above others:
Or as if I had a special faith above others:
Or as if I preached a different Gospel than
the apostles: Or as if I wished to have a special
God and Christ. There is only one God /

<summary>Valentin Weigel introduces his work, explaining that while he calls it "his" theology, it is actually the universal faith of the Bible, intended to prove he is not a heretic or sectarian leader.</summary>

--- Page 5 ---
<meta>This page continues Weigel's defense of his terminology. He moves from the general validity of his faith to the personal nature of spiritual experience, arguing that a "common" gift like the sun or God becomes "mine" when personally received.</meta>

one faith / one baptism / one Christ / one Gospel /
not two / not three. Rather, I call it *my theology* <note>original: "meam Theologiam"</note> because God has given it to me—as He has to all believers—as my own; just as we otherwise say: my God / my Christ / my life. As much as one has of God within him according to true knowledge: so much God is and remains his own / although He is a common Good for all / just as Dionysius the Areopagita <note>A 5th-century Christian philosopher whose writings on the "mystical union" with God were highly influential for Weigel and other reformers seeking an internal, rather than purely institutional, faith.</note> says: *As much good as one possesses, so much he also possesses of God* <note>original: "Quantum quis habet boni, tantum habet & DEI."</note>. That is: As much as one has of the Eternal Good / so much is God his own. As much as the sun shines on a person / so much may he say / My sun / even though it shines impartially on all others as well. My Christ / as much as one has of Christ and Christ’s life within him through faith / so much is Christ his own / here in time and there in eternity / even though He remains a common Savior of the whole world / He is a light for all people. My faith / as much as I have of faith / so much is that faith mine / even though it is the common gift of all Christians. Thus also my confession of faith / of theology <note>original: "Theologia"</note> / in which I intend with God’s help to stand / to go / to walk / which I preach as long as I live / that is my theology <note>original: "Theologia"</note> / my Gospel / my faith / which I confess before the world and publicly in my sermons / 
<margin>33.</margin>
now into the 33rd year / until this very day on which I write this / namely on the 23rd of December in the year 1594 <note>original: "Anno 1594"</note>. Another person may also do the same / confess and say: This is my theology <note>original: "Theologia"</note> / this is how I understand God’s Word / the articles of the whole

<summary>Weigel argues that although Christian truth is universal, it must be claimed as a personal possession ("my theology") just as one claims the sun's light. He notes that this has been his consistent public confession for thirty-three years, dating this preface to December 1594.</summary>
<keywords>personal faith, Dionysius the Areopagita, inner light, spiritual possession, 1594, confession of faith, religious experience</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Weigel's transition from defining his faith as "personal" to a formal defense against his critics. He previously explained that the truth of God becomes "mine" when it is lived internally; here, he identifies this personal theology with the traditional foundations of the Apostles while recounting his dissatisfaction with academic learning.</meta>

# Preface.

Religion / thus I believe / thus I preach / this is
nothing more than Jesus Christ crucified <note>original: "Jesum Christum crucifixum"</note>.

Let this be said of Weigelian theology <note>original: "Theologia VVeigelij"</note>,
that is, of the word "Mine" <note>Weigel is responding to critics who accused him of arrogance for calling his teachings "my theology." He argues that truth must be personally possessed to be real.</note>, from which my
adversaries here in this time and there in the world to come shall
see and grasp what my theology was,
and what theirs is; for I must be their
fanatic <term>Schwärmer</term> <note>A derogatory term used at the time for those believed to be "enthusiasts" who followed their own spirit rather than external church authority.</note>, deceiver, sacramentarian <term>Sacramentirer</term> <note>A label for those who held a symbolic rather than literal view of the bread and wine in the Lord's Supper.</note>, and
heretic; which I should indeed suffer
with Christ without offering a defense:
yet so that the simple-hearted are not offended by my speech,
but rather remain confirmed in the recognized truth, and so that I
may publicly confess myself to the unshakable
foundation of the Prophets and Apostles,
where Jesus Christ crucified and resurrected <note>original: "crucifixus & resuscitatus"</note> remains the cornerstone.
Thus I may boldly write and say: This is
my theology <note>original: "Theologia"</note>, thus I believe / thus I hold to
the twelve articles of the Apostles <note>The Apostles' Creed, the fundamental statement of Christian belief.</note> in the Holy Religion /
as a member of the universal, Catholic <note>Weigel uses "Catholic" here in its original sense of "universal" or "all-encompassing," rather than referring specifically to the Roman Catholic Church.</note>, non-partisan Church,
which is gathered in one
Spirit, Baptism, and Faith throughout the whole world.

*But how I came to the right, true, and unshakable foundation*
*of theology, hear now:*

I had studied for many years in the universities <note>original: "hohen Schulen"</note>
and in the books of men, and found no
true foundation among them all, neither
in theology <note>original: "Theologia"</note> nor in any other faculty; then I
turned myself to the LORD / and begged Him for understanding /
and soon He showed me the right way to the Holy
Scripture, which is a beginning, middle, and end
of all heavenly and earthly wisdom; He gave

He describes his spiritual journey from the emptiness of academic university study to finding true wisdom through direct prayer to God and the Holy Scriptures.</summary>

Here, he pivots to the core method of his spiritual discovery: the "inward turn" and the ancient maxim "Know Thyself," arguing that all true science and religion begin with self-knowledge.</meta>

...thus the kind God [showed] me the right way (I say)
so that I should learn to know myself and God
in Jesus Christ / in these three were Heaven and

->Know yourself is the highest and most<-
->necessary [task].<-

"Know thyself" <note>original Greek: "γνῶθι σεαυτὸν"</note> descends from heaven as a divine [command] <note>original Latin: "Labitur de coelo divinum"</note> as the saying goes / rightly and justly. I went into myself / there I found God and Christ in myself / and we three were One / and are One / and remain One in eternity. O Man, learn to know yourself and God / then you will have enough here and there of wisdom and salvation / for I may say / that <term>true Theology</term> <note>original: "vera Theologia"</note> is nothing else / than <term>to know oneself and GOD in JESUS CHRIST</term> <note>original: "cognoscere se ipsum & DEUM in JESU CHRISTO"</note>. Yes / <term>true Theology, Philosophy, Magic, Alchemy</term> <note>In Weigel’s time, these were not just "spells" or "science" but a unified way of understanding how the spiritual and physical worlds reflect one another.</note>, is to know oneself and God in Christ Jesus. If one wishes to be a true <term>Theologian</term> without error / let him know himself and God in Jesus Christ. If one wishes to be a true <term>Philosopher</term> / let him know himself and God in Jesus Christ. If one wishes to be a true <term>Magus</term> <note>original: "Magus"; Weigel refers to a spiritual practitioner of "Natural Magic"—understanding the hidden powers of God in nature—not a sorcerer.</note> / let him know himself and God in Jesus Christ (I do not mean here a sorcerer or a practitioner of the black arts). If one wishes to be a true <term>Alchemist</term> / (I do not mean a <term>toad-peddler</term> <note>original: "Vncken Krämer"; a derogatory term for fraudulent charlatans or "puffers" who only cared about making physical gold through deception.</note>) let him know himself and GOD in Christ Jesus. Likewise also a <term>Physician</term>, a <term>Jurist</term> / for we are indeed in the New Testament / and not in the Old.

<summary>Weigel argues that all true knowledge—whether in theology, science, or law—begins with the mystical "inward turn" to know oneself and God simultaneously. He redefines traditional academic disciplines as spiritual pursuits centered on the presence of Christ within the individual.</summary>

<keywords>Know Thyself, self-knowledge, mysticism, true theology, alchemy, magic, New Testament, internalism, spiritual unity</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Weigel's introduction to "Self-Knowledge." On the previous page, he established that true Theology and Philosophy are found by looking inward. # Preface.

<meta>catchword: created.</meta>

O Man, you should not cease to wonder at yourself / that you are so wonderfully made by your Creator. You are composed of two parts / of the eternal and of the temporal / of the heavenly and of the earthly. Therefore, you also carry a twofold knowledge or wisdom within you / for the eternal and for the temporal life / you are composed of spirit and flesh; these two make a whole / just as God and the created being / these both make one whole God <note>This is a characteristic Weigelian idea: that God and the human spirit are so intimately linked that they form a spiritual unity.</note>. The spirit is the head and the husband. The body is the wife / this is a *lawful, indissoluble marriage* <note>original Latin: "conjugium legitimum indissolubile"</note>, as long as the husband remains or is the head / [it is] *unlawful and dissoluble* <note>original Latin: "illegitimum dissolubile"</note>, where the woman rules / Such is called and is a false marriage / in which GOD has no pleasure. The Apostle
<margin>Ephesians 2</margin>
says: Remember / that you were Gentiles / without Christ / strangers and outside the citizenship of Israel / and strangers from the testaments of promise / therefore you had no hope / and were without God in the world. But now you who are in Christ and were once far off / have now been brought near through the blood of CHRIST / for He is our peace who has made one out of both / and has broken down the fence that was between them / in that HE through his flesh took away the enmity / Namely / the law, which was set in commandments / so that HE might [create] out of the two one new man in himself—

<summary>Weigel describes the human being as a dual creature of spirit and flesh, using the metaphor of marriage to illustrate the proper hierarchy of the soul. He cites Ephesians to argue that Christ reconciles these internal divisions to create a "new man."</summary>

# Preface.

[sunk] in <note>This continues from the previous page, referring to the "way" God has placed within the author.</note> / in which all Scripture is enclosed, contained, found, and understood. For the stone which the builders rejected has become the cornerstone, and this has been done by the Lord, and it is a marvel before our eyes: Namely, that we humans, through the **union of opposites** <note>original Latin: *combinationem contradictoriorum*</note>—that is, through Jesus Christ, [who is both] GOD and man—must and shall learn all earthly and heavenly wisdom: such as **Theology, Jurisprudence, Medicine, Philosophy, Magic**, etc. This is the day that the Lord has made; let us rejoice and be glad in it, namely in Christ, who is the Day and the Light, in whom we should study and learn **Theology**, and not from the **Church Fathers** <note>original Latin: *Patribus*</note> or **Commentaries**. He is the Light, the Way from which we shall take **Jurisprudence** [Law], and not **from Bartolus or Baldus**, etc. <note>Bartolus de Saxoferrato and Baldus de Ubaldis were the most influential medieval legal scholars; Weigel is arguing for spiritual insight over their complex legal codes.</note> He is the Way, Day, and Light, from which we shall learn **Kabbalah** and **Magic**, and not from **Pietro d'Abano** or **Cornelius Agrippa**. <note>These were the standard Renaissance authorities on occult and natural magic.</note> He is the Way, Day, and Light, from which we should study and learn **Physics** and **Philosophy**, and not from **Aristotle** or **Pliny**. <note>Aristotle's logic and Pliny’s natural history were the foundations of the university curriculum at the time.</note> In this one Jesus Christ, who hung on the Cross and died for our sins, etc., we should rejoice and be satisfied; there we find full sufficiency and wisdom for all faculties, arts, and languages; and through Him we come to the Father, and remain with Him eternally blessed, for which purpose we were in the beginning

->[and] are redeemed.<-

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the dual nature of man. While the previous page established that the human being is a "marriage" of the eternal spirit and the temporal body, this chapter transitions into the necessity of reconciling these opposites to achieve true understanding of God and the self.</meta>

Public Faith
C z

## Chapter 1.

### Concerning the Order of this Counsel according to the Beginning, Middle, and End.

Mankind is created, redeemed, and sealed with the Holy Spirit for this purpose: that in this [temporal] time, he should study, learn, and unite two contrary things—not only through the distinction of <term>Reason <note>original Latin: "per Rationem"</note></term>, but also through the essential union of the <term>Mind <note>original Latin: "per Mentem"; in this context, "Mind" refers to a higher spiritual faculty of direct intuition rather than mere logical calculation.</note></term>. For example: God hears all things; God hears nothing. Likewise: God regrets nothing, and yet God regrets <note>This refers to the theological paradox where the Bible describes God as unchanging, yet also says He "repented" or "was grieved" (e.g., Genesis 6:6).</note>.

Therefore, two contrary things are united and recognized as true because one must take together Alpha and Omega <note>original Greek: α & ω; the beginning and the end.</note>, beginning and end, eternity and time, heaven and earth, God and creature, the spirit and the letter, the inner and the outer. Otherwise, one could not attain any certain or perfect knowledge of all things. For example: God hears nothing because He hears all things; God hears all because He hears nothing. Likewise: God regrets because He regrets nothing, and God regrets nothing because He regrets.

Therefore, man must and shall unite Alpha and Omega, eternity and time—<term>as will be said later <note>original Latin: "ut post dicetur"</note></term>—so that neither can exist without the other. For instance, God’s hearing in time would be of no use without God’s not-hearing in eternity, and His eternal not-hearing would be of no use without His temporal hearing. Likewise: the Divine regret in time would be of no use without His lack of regret in eternity.

<summary>This page introduces the necessity of reconciling spiritual paradoxes, arguing that true knowledge requires uniting opposites like eternity and time or the inner and outer self. Weigel suggests that humans are uniquely created to bridge these contradictions through both logical reason and spiritual intuition.</summary>
<keywords>paradox, Alpha and Omega, eternity and time, Reason vs Mind, divine hearing, divine regret, Valentin Weigel, reconciliation of opposites</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument from the previous page regarding the paradoxical nature of God’s attributes, specifically how "regret" and "hearing" function differently in the realms of time and eternity. It moves into a broader philosophical discussion on the necessity of opposites (contraries) for the existence of the world.</meta>

<header>Confession [of Faith].</header>

eternity, and His temporal regret could not occur without His non-regret.

<margin>D4</margin>
Therefore, neither can exist or be perfect without the other. Daily experience testifies to this, showing how the temporal world could not remain or exist without opposing things:

*The perfection of the world consists in contraries.* <note>original Latin: "Perfectio Mundi consistit in contrarijs."</note> For example: if there were only day without night, or *the reverse*; only heat without cold: *If all people were rich and none were poor; and so it is with all contraries in the world, as will be explained later at greater length in the recapitulation.*

<margin>E5</margin>
Therefore, daily experience testifies to such contraries in the temporal world so that we might elevate ourselves during our bodily life from the visible to the invisible, from time to eternity, from the perishable to the imperishable, from the letter to the spirit, from the <term>creature <note>Refers to any created thing or being in the universe.</note></term> to the <term>Master Builder <note>original German: "Werckmeister"; a common term for God as the architect of creation.</note></term>. This is achieved not by casting away <term>otherness <note>original Latin: "alteritatis"; the state of being different or distinct.</note></term>, but through the unification of the heavenly with the earthly. This union teaches and testifies to us that in the heavenly-eternal realm there are also contraries, but they are united through the <term>Mind <note>original Latin: "mentem"; used here to mean a higher spiritual faculty of intuition that can grasp paradoxes.</note></term>, just as these same things in time are separated by <term>Reason <note>original German: "Vernunfft"; the logical faculty that distinguishes and divides things.</note></term>. For example: God’s hearing is a non-hearing, for time has become eternity; *according to the principle that lower things exist within their superiors according to the mode of the superior, not as they are in themselves—for lower things have a nobler and more transparent existence within their superiors than they do in themselves.* <note>original Latin: "Inferiora sunt in suis superioribus secundum modum superiorum non ut in se - Nam inferiora nobilius & perspectius esse habent in suis superioribus quam in seipsis." This is a standard Neoplatonic and Scholastic axiom.</note> God’s non-hearing is a hearing, for eternity is time, God is creature, our hearing is God’s hearing, and God’s hearing is our hearing. God regrets, for time is eternity; and God does not regret, for eternity is time.

<margin>F6</margin>
Therefore, one can, should, and must elevate oneself

<summary>This page argues that the "perfection of the world" requires the existence of opposites, such as heat and cold or wealth and poverty. It explains that while human Reason separates these opposites, the higher spiritual Mind can unite them, allowing a person to see how God can simultaneously experience temporal change and eternal constancy.</summary>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page establishes the foundational premise of the work, continuing the discussion on how divine attributes that appear contradictory—such as God’s hearing and His silence, or His regret and His immutability—must be reconciled through a higher spiritual understanding that bridges time and eternity.</meta>

<header>Public Confession</header>

# Of the Order of this Counsel according to the Beginning, Middle, and End.
## The 1st Chapter.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial letter 'D' featuring ornate scrollwork and floral motifs.</image-desc>
**Man** is created, redeemed, and sealed with the Holy Spirit for this purpose: that he should study, learn, and unify two contrary things during this life—not only through the separation by **Reason** <note>original Latin: *per Rationem*; refers to logical, discursive thought</note>, but also through the essential unification by the **Mind** <note>original Latin: *per Mentem*; refers to a higher, intuitive spiritual faculty</note>. For instance: God hears all things, yet God hears nothing. Likewise: Nothing causes God regret, and yet God feels regret.

Therefore, two contrary things are unified and recognized as true because one must bring together the Alpha and Omega <note>Greek: α & ω; the first and last letters of the alphabet, symbolizing the totality of the beginning and the end</note>, beginning and end, eternity and time, heaven and earth, God and <term>creature <note>Refers to any created being or thing in the universe, as distinct from the Creator.</note></term>, the spirit and the letter, the inner and the outer. Otherwise, one could not attain any certain or perfect knowledge of all things. For example: God hears nothing, because He hears all things; God hears all, because He hears nothing. Likewise: God regrets, because He regrets nothing; and God regrets nothing, because He regrets.

Therefore, man must and shall unify the Alpha and Omega, eternity and time—**as will be said later** <note>original Latin: *ut post dicetur*</note>—because neither can exist without the other. For instance, God’s hearing within time would be of no use without God’s not-hearing in eternity, and His eternal not-hearing would be of no use without His temporal hearing. Likewise: Divine regret in time would be of no use without His lack of regret in eternity.

<sig>C 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: eternity /</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the theological necessity of unifying opposites, arguing that true knowledge of God requires reconciling His eternal, unchanging nature with His active, temporal relationship with the world. It frames this "unification of contraries" as the primary purpose of human study and spiritual growth.</summary>
<keywords>paradox, divine attributes, Alpha and Omega, eternity and time, Reason vs. Mind, spiritual epistemology, God's regret, hearing of God</keywords>

It specifically addresses how the contradictions seen in the physical world (like day and night) are necessary for perfection and how these opposites are unified in the divine "Mind" where time and eternity become one.</meta>

<header>Confession.</header>

...eternity, and His temporal regret could not occur without His non-regret.

<margin>D4</margin>
For this reason, however, neither can exist nor be perfect without the other, as daily experience testifies: how the temporal world could neither remain nor exist without **contrary things**:

*The perfection of the world consists in contraries.* <note>original Latin: *Perfectio Mundi consistit in contrariis.*</note> For instance: if there were only day without night, or vice versa <note>original Latin: *è contra*</note>; or only heat without cold; or if all people were rich and there were no poor; and so with all contraries in the world, as will be explained more broadly below in the recapitulation <note>original Latin: *sic de omnibus in mundo contrariis, ut infrà latius in recapitulatione*</note>.

<margin>E5.</margin>
Therefore, daily experience testifies to such contraries <note>original: *contraria*</note> in the temporal world, so that during our physical life we might raise ourselves from the visible to the invisible, from time to eternity, from the perishable to the imperishable, from the letter to the spirit, from the <term>creature <note>Refers to any created being or thing, distinguished from the Creator.</note></term> to the <term>Master Craftsman <note>original German: *Werckmeister*; a term for God as the architect or artisan of the universe.</note></term>—not by casting aside otherness <note>original Latin: *alteritatis*; the state of being different or "other."</note>, but through the unification of the heavenly with the earthly. This unification instructs and testifies to us that in the heavenly eternal there are also contraries, yet they are unified through the **Mind** <note>original Latin: *mentem*; referring to the higher spiritual faculty of intuition.</note>, just as these things in time are separated by **Reason** <note>original German: *Vernunfft*; referring to logical, discursive thought that categorizes and divides.</note>. For instance: God’s hearing is a non-hearing, for time has become eternity—according to the saying: *Lower things are in their higher counterparts according to the manner of the higher, not as they are in themselves—for lower things have a nobler and more perfect existence in their higher counterparts than in themselves.* <note>original Latin: *Inferiora sunt in suis superioribus secundum modum superiorum non ut in se- Nam inferiora nobilius & perfectius esse habent in suis superioribus quam in seipsis.*</note> God’s non-hearing is a hearing, for eternity is time; God is <term>creature</term>; our hearing is God’s hearing, and God’s hearing is our hearing. God regrets, for time is eternity; and God does not regret, for eternity is time.

<margin>F6.</margin>
Therefore, one can, shall, and must raise oneself

# Public Faith

...bring one from the transitory to the intransitory and from time to eternity, so that a **distinction** <note>original Latin: *discrimen*</note> is maintained between **Knowledge and the Schools** <note>original Latin: *Cognitionis & Scholarum*</note>. In natural knowledge, one separates all things **according to the light of the Dialecticians** <note>original Latin: *juxta lumen Dialecticorum*; referring to the traditional logic and reasoning used in academic philosophy.</note>, through reason and **through discourse** <note>original Latin: *per discursum*</note>. Supernatural knowledge unites all things because the **Mind** <note>original Latin: *Mentem*</note> in the heavenly realm is the **union of things** <note>original Latin: *unio rerum*</note>. All things are one; however, in earthly things there is a **distinction of things** <note>original Latin: *discrimen rerum*</note>, where all things are separated **according to beings, places, and times** <note>original Latin: *juxta entia; loca, tempora*</note>.

Thus, there are also three schools for learning all things:

1.  **The first and lowest school** is sensory, where one creature learns from another, believes based on good persuasion, and one reads to another from a book; this is called the **Human School**. For example: "God hears all things."
2.  **The second school** is the **Light of Nature** <note>original Latin: *Lumen Naturæ*; the ability to understand the world through human reason and observation.</note>, where two **contradictory things** <note>original Latin: *contradictoria*</note> are held side by side but are not united; here, one part is **absolutely true** <note>original Latin: *absolutè verum*</note> and the other is true only **relatively** <note>original Latin: *per respectum*</note>. For example: "God hears nothing," or "God regrets nothing"—the opposite remains a lie to this school.
3.  **The third school** is the **Light of Grace** <note>original Latin: *Lumen Gratiæ*; a higher, divinely inspired level of understanding.</note>, where the **Mind** <note>original Latin: *Mens*</note> unites two opposing things. For example: "God hears all things because He hears nothing," and "God hears nothing because He hears everything."

<sig>G 7.</sig>

Therefore, one must maintain a distinction **of knowledge and of the schools** <note>original Latin: *cognitionis & Scholarum*</note> so that the Holy Trinity may be rightly recognized according to the three physical persons in time, and according to the three eternal persons in the Heavenly Being.

Rational knowledge separates the persons from one another (as is proper): the Father has one office, the Son has another, and the Holy Spirit has another; yet there is only one God. Reason **disputes for and against** <note>original Latin: *disputiert pro & contra*</note>: "the Holy Spirit is a creature," or "the Holy Spirit is not a creature."

But the **Mind** <note>original Latin: *Mens*</note> or Faith, which is above all reason, 
<meta>catchword: recognizes</meta>

<summary>The author defines three levels of understanding—sensory, rational (Light of Nature), and spiritual (Light of Grace)—concluding that while reason separates divine attributes, higher faith unites them. This framework is used to explain the mystery of the Trinity and the reconciliation of seemingly contradictory theological statements.</summary>

<keywords>Trinity, Light of Nature, Light of Grace, Mind, three schools of learning, logic, dialectic, divine unity, contradictions</keywords>

<header>Confession.</header>

...recognizes both as true. He is the surrendered serenity <note>original: *die gelassene Gelassenheit*; a key concept in German mysticism referring to the total letting go of the self and the will to God</note> through the Cross in Christ Jesus; there, no disputing exists, and there are no **religious dissensions** <note>original Latin: *dissidia in religione*</note>.

Therefore, a person should and must rightly recognize the Holy Trinity, both heavenly and earthly, so that they may unite with what is recognized and become **deified** <note>original: *vergottet*; the mystical process of the soul's union with God</note>, a child of God born out of God Himself. Thus, they may remain and walk in the unity of the Father, in the freedom of the Son, and in the perfection of the <term>Holy Spirit</term>, and even while in the physical body, be able to enter at all hours of the night and day into the **holy of holies** <note>original Latin: *sanctorum sanctum*</note> and hear and learn all things in **silence and Sabbath rest** <note>original Latin: *silentio & sabbatho*</note> about which nothing can be spoken or written; let each experience it for themselves, for no one can go to heaven or hell on behalf of another.

### Chapter 2.

## That for this reason Man was created, redeemed, and sealed with God’s Spirit: that he should unite <term>two contradictory things</term> <note>original Latin: *duo contradictoria*</note>.

It seems strange to the simple <term>theologian</term> <note>original Latin: *Theologo*</note>, as well as to the rational master of scripture skilled in languages, that man was created, redeemed, and sanctified for this very purpose: to unite two opposing things and recognize them as true, so that in him "yes" and "no" become one and the same thing. We know that an unlearned **layman** <note>original Latin: *idiota*; here meaning a common person without formal academic training</note> cannot manage to unite two opposing things, for he allows his worldly reason to dominate him. Likewise, we know that all the world-scholars, through their **logical discourse** <note>original Latin: *discursum*</note> of reason, cannot allow "yes" and "no" to coexist—that is, to unite <term>two contradictory things</term> <note>original Latin: *duo contradictoria*</note>—otherwise they would have to [abandon] their

### Public Confession

...lay aside the **light of logic** <note>original Latin: *lumen Dialecticum*; the use of human dialectics or formal logic to understand God</note>; instead, one part is <unclear>truth</unclear> to them in an absolute sense, while the opposite is a lie to them in a relative sense <note>original Latin: *per respectum*</note>. Thus, they resist the Holy Spirit with their clever **reason** <term>Reason</term> <note>original: *Vernunfft*; in this mystical context, it refers to human intellectual pride which stands in opposition to divine revelation</note> as enemies of God and His Christ. They stumble at the Gospel and dispute against it—namely, against faith in Jesus Christ. Reason fights against faith; it refuses to trust in the future, where God Himself will teach, comfort, and enlighten. Such <term>theologians</term> <note>original: *Theologi*</note> thus reveal that they are deprived of holy faith and have never recognized Jesus Christ with His holy Cross.

Now I say here in my <term>theology</term> <note>original: *Theologia*</note> that we humans are all created by God for this purpose, redeemed through Christ, and anointed with the Holy Spirit for this: that we should and must unite **two contradictory things** <note>original Latin: *duo contradictoria*</note> and recognize them as true. That is, we must enter into faith through **Baptism** <note>original: *Tauff*</note> and the **Lord's Supper** <note>original: *Nachtmahl*</note>, surrender ourselves to God, and allow ourselves to be put to death and buried with Christ, to rise with Him to a new life. This means to extinguish our sinful, fleshly lust, to subdue and kill our **reason**, to turn ourselves around and become like children. 

It is as often said: the old, natural man can neither understand nor grasp divine things—namely, that which is beyond the grasp and comprehension of our reason must be committed to faith in Jesus Christ. The "rational ones" dispute over what faith is, and one brands the other a heretic over things they themselves neither know nor possess. A person must

<margin>/</margin> Spirit; it does not consist in clever, rational knowledge,

# Confession.

God hears all things / God hears nothing: both are true at once, and both are found together in the Scripture. The sensory man knows nothing of this / the rational man disputes for and against it / he recognizes only one part as true / the <term>Mental Man</term> <note>original: *Mentalis*; referring to a person who perceives through the higher spirit or "mind of Christ" rather than through physical senses or human logic</note> believes and recognizes both as true / just as it is true in itself / and thereby he is blessed / for he is one with CHRIST through the Cross / in which he falls away from his sensuality and recognizes that he cannot reach or grasp it with his sharp reason or mastery / he despairs of his own strength / crawls to the Cross / <note>original Latin: *mortuus carni, sanguini, Mundo*</note> dead to the flesh, to blood, and to the world; His life is hidden in God in Christ / through this death of CHRIST within him, God’s hearing in time is united with His not-hearing in eternity / and the regret of GOD <note>In many mystical texts, "God’s regret" refers to biblical passages where God is said to "repent" or change His mind (as in the story of Jonah), contrasted with His eternal, unchanging nature.</note> with His lack of regret / that is / he becomes a newly born person out of GOD, a true Christian / nature is drawn into grace / the earth brought into heaven / time brought to eternity / so that these two become one thing / for Christ can do this with His Cross / that out of the old and new man, one thing is made through Himself / Ephesians 2. God has become everything in the person / thus our ears are God’s ears / they hear what God wills / our eyes are God’s eyes / they see what God wills / and not what we will / our heart is God’s heart / it regrets when He wills / and not when we will. Therefore, God's hearing is a not-hearing / and God's not-hearing is a hearing: there is the <term>union of God and man</term> <note>original Latin: *unio DEI & hominis*</note> through Jesus Christ, there is God’s hut / dwelling / temple / <term>tabernacle</term> <note>original: *Tabernacul*; a dwelling place or tent, specifically referring to the portable earthly dwelling of God's presence</note>, there is <term>Christ dwelling essentially</term> <note>original Latin: *Christus inhabitans essentialiter*; the belief that Christ does not just "count" the believer as righteous, but actually lives his very essence inside the person</note>, God dwells bodily in the person and the person in God, not through im- <note>The text cuts off here; the catchword *putationem* indicates the full word is "imputation," referring to a theological debate over whether righteousness is merely assigned to a person or actually inherent within them.</note>

Reason fights against faith,

when God Himself will teach,

# Profession of Faith.
<meta>catchword: imputation</meta>

God hears all things; God hears nothing: both of these are true at once, and both are found together in the Scriptures. The <term>sensual man</term> <note>original: *Sinnliche*; referring to a person governed by physical senses and worldly desires rather than spiritual insight.</note> knows nothing of this; the rational man disputes <term>for and against</term> <note>original: *pro & contra*</note> it, recognizing only one part as true. However, the <term>spiritual man</term> <note>original: *Mentalis*; one who possesses a higher, divinely-illumined mind.</note> believes and recognizes both as true, just as it is true in himself. Through this he is blessed, for he is one with CHRIST through the Cross, in which he falls away from his sensuality and recognizes that he cannot reach or grasp it with his sharp Reason or mastery. He despairs of his own power and crawls to the Cross, <term>dead to the flesh, blood, and the World</term> <note>original: *mortuus carni, sanguini, Mundo*</note>. His life is hidden in God in Christ. Through this death of CHRIST within him, God’s hearing in time is united with His not-hearing in eternity, and God’s repenting is united with His not-repenting. That is, he becomes a newborn person out of GOD, a true Christian. Nature is drawn into grace, earth is brought into heaven, and time into eternity, so that these two become one thing. For Christ can do this with His Cross: that out of the old and new man, one single thing is made through Himself (Ephesians 2). God has become everything in the person; thus our ears are God’s ears—they hear what God wills; our eyes are God’s eyes—they see what God wills and not what we will; our heart is God’s heart—He repents when He wills, and not when we will. Therefore, God’s hearing is a not-hearing, and God’s not-hearing is a hearing. There is the <term>union of GOD and man through Jesus Christ</term> <note>original: *unio DEI & hominis per Jesum Christum*</note>. There is God’s hut, dwelling, temple, and <term>Tabernacle</term> <note>original: *Tabernacul*</note>. There is Christ <term>dwelling within essentially</term> <note>original: *inhabitans essentialiter*; expressing the belief that Christ inhabits the believer's actual substance or essence, rather than being a distant influence.</note>. God dwells bodily in the person and the person in God, not through <term>imputation</term> <note>original: *imputationem*; the theological concept of being declared righteous as a legal status, which the author contrasts here with an actual, physical indwelling.</note>

<summary>This page explains the mystical union between the believer and God, where human senses and reason are surrendered to allow Christ to dwell "essentially" within the person. It resolves biblical paradoxes by arguing that the true Christian becomes a vessel through which God's own will, hearing, and sight operate.</summary>
<keywords>mystical union, indwelling of Christ, paradox of faith, Reason vs. Spirit, internal transformation, Tabernacle, imputation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the argument that humans must surrender their self-will to God. It bridges the transition from the necessity of spiritual "death" to the self to the definition of true theology as the union of opposites—the Beginning (Alpha) and the End (Omega)—which the rational mind cannot comprehend.</meta>

# Profession of Faith

...[should not] belong to ourselves, but to Him by whom we were created; *for otherness cannot suffice for you;* <note>original: *alteritas enim tibi sufficere non potest*</note> We should let God be everything in us; we should put off the old man and put on the new; we should walk in <term>Gelassenheit</term> <note>A central term in German mysticism meaning "yieldedness," "serenity," or the total surrender of the self-will to God.</note>; we should not claim anything for ourselves; we should walk in Love; we should remain *in unity and liberty* <note>original: *in unitate & libertate*</note>; Now when we—I say—walk in faith, then we must confess what is necessary: *to die to the flesh, the blood, the world, and the whole animalistic man;* <note>original: *mori carni, sanguini, mundo & toti homini animali*</note> that is, the sensory flesh ceases, Reason allows itself to be swallowed up into the <term>Mind</term> <note>original: *Mentem*; here referring to the higher spiritual intellect or the spark of the soul.</note>, and the Mind is united with God. These are *two contradictory things* <note>original: *duo contradictoria*</note>; yet for this purpose, man is born, redeemed, and sealed through the Holy Spirit.

# Chapter 3.
## That one must unite *two contradictory things*
## because the *Alpha and Omega* <note>original Greek: *α & ω*</note>, Beginning and End
## must be taken together.

True *Theology* is not a sensory believing based on mere persuasion, nor is it a rational mastery of Holy Scripture, where one uses Reason to separate truth from lies and can never unite *two opposites* <note>original: *duo contraria*</note>; rather, true *Theology* is where the End and the Beginning are taken and grasped together. Namely, through Holy Faith, which trusts God without any searching, brooding, or discoursing; where one trusts in faith alone without any human searching, where only the *Mind* <note>original: *Mens*</note> and not *Reason* <note>original: *Ratio*</note> rules. For *Reason* is not that simple faith in *Jesus Christ*, because Reason always fights against faith,

...[things] against God and cannot believe; that is, maintaining "yes" and "no" as opposites. For if one wishes to have Christ through faith, he must have the whole—that is, the End with the Beginning—just as the Cross unifies Time with Eternity. Christ is God and the Word; He is the Old and New Testament; that is, in Him the Old Testament is brought into the New, so that they both are One.

### Chapter 4.
# That by necessity one must
## unify the Beginning with the End.

Man is created, redeemed, and sanctified for this purpose: to unify two things. To achieve this, it is required that he joins the Beginning and the End together, for one cannot exist without the other. For example: the Beginning is not without the End; the End is not without the Beginning. Time cannot exist, nor persist, without Eternity; and Eternity is not entirely without Time. God is not without the Word, and the Word is not without God; rather, God is the Word, and the Word is God—these two make God complete. The man is only half, the woman is only half, but these two make a whole; the woman does not give birth without the man, and the...

Man is not without the woman / but these two together bring forth the fruit. In the same way, Eternity is the man / Time is the woman / she must become pregnant by Eternity / Eternity is of no use without Time / and Time can bring forth nothing without Eternity. Thus, what would Heaven be worth without the Earth / and what could the Earth bring forth without Heaven? Nothing at all. Neither can exist without the other. If you say: "Eternity can indeed exist / without Time / **GOD** can indeed exist without the creature / just as the Eternal **GOD** existed before the temporal creature?" 

Answer: You do not have a whole knowledge, but a half-fragmented one / we imagine how **GOD** was before the world was. Likewise / we imagine how Eternity exists without Time and before Time: although that is partly and naturally true and right / yet for perfect knowledge the **opposite** <note>original: *oppositum*</note> must also be as true as that / and a **union** <note>original: *unio*</note> must occur **in reality** <note>original: *realiter*</note> so that the **Alpha and Omega** <note>original: α & ω</note>, Eternity and Time, are together without beginning / equal with **GOD** / and that the Beginning is not without the End / just as the End is not without the Beginning / Eternity not without Time / and Time not without Eternity.

If one wishes to have a whole knowledge in **theology** <note>original: *Theologia*</note> and thereafter in all things / then it is necessary to bring the End to the Beginning / and to make the Beginning out of the End / and the End out of the Beginning. Whoever cannot unify **two contradictions** <note>original: *duo contradictoria*</note> and recognize them as true / **in themselves in one principle** <note>original: *per se in uno principio*</note> / does not yet know the Beginning and the End / Eternity and Time / **GOD** and the creature must be taken together / toward true— <note>The text breaks here; the catchword "ram" suggests the following page begins with the completion of the Latin word "veram" (true).</note>

-># Profession of Faith.<-

*...true and perfect knowledge.* <note>original: *ram & perfectam cognitionem*</note> Indeed, whoever does not unite Eternity and Time / Beginning and End together **in reality** <note>original: *realiter*</note>, he has no **GOD**, is outside of **CHRIST**, and does not know that through the Cross all things must be brought together into one. One must bring the End into the Beginning / the Creature to Grace / the old man into the new / the letter to the spirit / so that out of the letter, the spirit may emerge. Adam cannot be without Christ / Christ not without Adam / the Spirit would be of no use without the Letter, and the Letter could not be without the Spirit. **Hermes Trismegistus** <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure traditionally associated with the fusion of the Greek god Hermes and the Egyptian god Thoth, often cited as a source of ancient divine wisdom.</note> also linked the Beginning with the End / that is, he possessed a **divine mental cognition** <note>original: *mentalem divinam cognitionem*</note> / which unites two things that are otherwise contrary **in themselves** <note>original: *per se*</note> / when he says to **Asclepius**: "When you wish to understand all things, remember two; remember indeed the Begetter and the Begotten, and remember that these are the universe." <note>original: *Cum omnia intelligere volueris, recordare duorum memento nempe Genitoris & Geniti eáq; esse universa memento.*</note> It is as if I were to speak with John: God and the Word are all things / outside of this there is nothing / neither in Heaven nor on Earth. If you know **GOD** and the Word / which has become Flesh / then you know **JESUS CHRIST**, Beginning and End and all things / therefore **Mercury** <note>Mercury is used here interchangeably with Hermes Trismegistus.</note> says further: "And nothing will be ambiguous to you, neither of the things above, nor the things below, whether divine, or mutable, or those that lie hidden in darkness: for these **TWO** are all things, namely the Begetter and the Begotten, nor can the one be separated from the other: for it is not possible for the begetter to be without the begotten, as each of them exists as that very thing. Therefore, neither may be disjoined from the other, just as neither can be divided from itself, since..." <note>original: *Nihilq; tibi erit ambiguum nec supernorum, nec infernorum, seu divinorum, seu mutabilium, aut eorum, quæ in tenebris delitescunt: DUO nempe cuncta sunt Genitor & Genitum, nec secerni alterum ab altero potest: neq; enim genitorem absq; genito esse possibile est, utrumq; illorū idipsum existit. Neither can be separated from the other / neither can exist without the other / it is impossible / one cannot divide one from the other / any more than one can separate a thing from itself: that is / these two are thus enclosed within one another / interwoven and essentially united / so that it is impossible that one could exist without the other / just as a creature <note>original: *Creatur*</note> cannot be without God the Creator / and the creator <note>original: *creans*</note> can be no Creator without the created <note>original: *creato*</note>; each of them exists as the selfsame thing. 

Each is that thing itself: For example / Time is Eternity / and Eternity is Time / the creature is God / and God is the creature / Just as that philosopher rightly said: "What I see and hear men do, that is God." If you abolish one of these two / you lose the other along with it / as Mercury <note>The Roman name for Hermes Trismegistus, a legendary figure representing ancient esoteric wisdom.</note> says: "Whoever takes away one of these, loses the other" <note>original: *Qui unum horum subtrahit, perdit & alterum*</note>: if you grant Time, you shall have Eternity; by granting the corporeal, the incorporeal is given; by the gift of the finite, the infinite shall be given. The old man <note>A theological reference to the unregenerate human nature.</note> cannot exist without the new / the inner man not without the outer / God not without the Word / and the Word not without God. 

All things are hidden in all things / <note>original: *Omnia sunt occultata in omnibus*</note>. This rule must be applied to the combination of contradictories or their essential union. Therefore, one should know very well / that Unity <note>original: *Vnitas*</note> exists in Otherness <note>original: *alteritate*</note> / like the light in the darkness / and that Otherness exists in Unity, like the darkness in the light /

<margin>How is the world eternal?</margin>
As eternity is in time and time is in eternity: God is in the creature and the creature is in God; the Word is in God and God is the Word; and eternity is time, God is creature, and as one is in the other, so is neither without the other and both are with one another. Just as God’s resting <note>original: "gerewen," likely an archaic form of "ruhen" (to rest/repose), referring to the divine Sabbath or stillness.</note> in the creature and time cannot be without His not-resting in eternity. God’s not-seeing in eternity cannot be without His seeing in time; for if God does not see, yet He sees, and if He sees, yet He does not see. <note>This refers to the mystical paradox that God is both beyond all perception (the "Divine Dark") and simultaneously perceives everything through His creation.</note> From this you may see that even though Scripture and natural knowledge <term>natural knowledge: human reason and observation of the physical world</term> establish that the world has a beginning, it is nevertheless, according to spiritual-intellectual divine knowledge, eternal without beginning, just as God is eternal without beginning. This is because <note>original: *quod neutrum sine altero esse non possit in cognitione divina*</note> neither can exist without the other in the divine knowledge. And although God is eternal without beginning, He has nevertheless taken a beginning with the world, for the world has a beginning and is created.

Thus it remains true: God is eternal and is not eternal; which is to say, God has no beginning because He has a beginning. The world is eternal without beginning because God is eternal without beginning; the world has a beginning, God has a beginning. What I previously rejected along with the worldly scholars out of ignorance, I now accept with a sound understanding and say: that the world is eternal just as God is eternal. God is the Creator <note>original: *seu unitas*</note> or Unity; the world is <note>original: *alteritas*</note> "otherness." Unity is in otherness; otherness is in unity. God is one, but Unity is not <note>original: *sine Mundo*</note> without the world. The world is also one unto itself, but not without God. These two are together; if God is eternal, then the world is also eternal without beginning, for the creature is not <note>original: *sine creante*</note> without the creator—it is with, in, and through Him. And the creator <note>original: *creans*</note> is not a creator without the creature, but rather the creator is in, with, and through

Thus, God also has a beginning; if time has a beginning, then eternity also has a beginning. If eternity is without beginning, then time is also without beginning.

The maker and the made <note>original: "Faciens & factum"</note> are one, for the thing made does not exist without a maker, and a maker does not exist without a thing made. The world was not made without God as the maker, but God the maker is eternal; therefore, the world is also eternal. The unbegotten <note>original: "Ingenitum"</note> does not exist without the begotten <note>original: "genito"</note>; the unbegotten is eternal and without beginning; therefore, the begotten is also without beginning and eternal. God and the Word are eternal without beginning; there is no "before" <note>original: "prius"</note> there, for it is eternity <note>original: "æternitas"</note>, yet it is not without time—otherwise there is no God and no Word. # Profession

The beginning / the creature is the end; the Word is the beginning / the creation is the end. Now the end is to be brought into the beginning, so that they both are united, such that the beginning is everything. Hence one says, God is Beginning and End. Christ is indeed Alpha and Omega <note>original: "$\alpha$ & $\omega$"</note>, for He is God Himself. And since God is Spirit, Person, and Word, He is Beginning, Middle, and End. Time is the last <note>original: "ultimum"</note>, and eternity is the first <note>original: "primum"</note>. There, time must be brought to eternity, so that eternity comes out of time, the beginning comes out of the end, grace comes out of nature, rest comes out of activity, and the three heavenly persons come out of the three earthly persons.

Whoever does not take Alpha and Omega <note>original: "$\alpha$ & $\omega$"</note> together and unite them in reality <note>original: "realiter"</note> can never crucify the Scripture. Whoever does not crucify the Scripture—that is <note>original: "h. e." for the Latin "hoc est"</note>, whoever cannot unify all contradictory verses through the Cross—cannot be a Doctor of Theology and will reach no perfect understanding throughout his life. For all Scripture is ordered toward the Apocalypse <note>The "unveiling" or the Book of Revelation</note>, which is to say, toward the Cross.

But you might ask: how can eternity or God not exist before the creature? For Mercury <note>The author refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure central to Hermetic philosophy.</note> and Boethius <note>An influential 6th-century philosopher known for bridging classical logic and Christian theology.</note> say: *God is prior to the world. If, therefore, two things are granted, the cause and the effect, the creator and the created, they are indeed united with each other; yet in such a way that one precedes the other, and the other follows. The one preceding is surely God the cause, and the one following is that which is the effect.*

**Answer:** They always speak in such a way that the opposite <note>original: "oppositum"</note> must not be left out. That "God is prior to the world" and "precedes the creature" is true according to the natural light <note>Human reason or physical observation.</note>. It is also true according to the mental divine light. But just as reason <note>original: "ratio"</note> cannot recognize the opposite as true, it is nevertheless true and easy for the spiritual Mind <note>original: "Menti"</note> to recognize both: that God the Creator is not "before" the creature, but rather both exist together. As God is eternal, so also is the world...

Eternal/ is the beginning of the world / so is God also with a

*God is prior to the world not by reason of time, but rather by the simplicity of nature* <note>original: "Deus est prior mundo non ratione temporis sed potius naturæ simplicitate"</note>, as indeed Boethius <note>An influential 6th-century philosopher</note> demonstrates with an example / from Plato: *If a foot had stood in the dust from eternity, then the foot and the footprint <note>original: "imago pedis"</note> would have existed together from eternity, and the foot would be prior to the footprint not by reason of time, but rather by the simplicity of nature.* <note>original: "Si pes ab æterno stetisset in pulvere tunc pes & imago pedis essent simul ab æterno, & pes esset prior imagine non temporis ratione sed potius naturæ simplicitate"</note> We imagine the heavens were created before the earth / and the invisible before the visible. Although such a thing is true according to the temporal, natural understanding / yet the other is also true / the heavens were created with the earth in a single point / the invisible with the visible / in a single glance / not one after another / this today / that tomorrow / etc. as Moses <note>The traditional author of the Book of Genesis</note> must rightly prescribe for common understanding. Therefore a twofold interpretation of Genesis <note>The first book of the Bible, concerning creation</note> is necessary: One natural and one supernatural heavenly: If there is an earthly [understanding] / so there is also a heavenly understanding of the works of God / neither is perfect without the other / Nature is of no use without Grace / and Grace is of no use without Nature / for whose Grace would Grace be? / God is not God without the creature / for whose **GOD** would He be? / therefore they both must be together / and neither is complete

## Chapter 6.

->**The opposites or contrary things in**<-
->**time should lift us up to the Eternal, where**<-
->**all things are one and imperishable.**<-

We see in this time and world / that opposites <note>original: "contraria"</note> must stand and exist alongside one another without mixing / and that one is always against the other / heat against cold / sweetness against sourness / death against life. ## Chapter 7.

### That one must properly and necessarily establish and maintain the *distinction between the two types of knowledge and the three schools* <note>original: *discrimen duplicis cognitionis & trium Scholarum*</note>.

Therefore, one must climb out of time toward eternity / out of the earthly toward the heavenly, and let oneself be led and lifted up *from imperfect otherness to perfect unity* <note>original: *ex alteritate imperfecta ad unitatem perfectam*. This refers to moving from the fragmented world of many things to the singular unity of God.</note>, so that one may distinguish and discuss the two-fold knowledge together with the three-fold schools / or for this reason one must necessarily maintain or establish a distinction *between the two-fold knowledge and the three-fold school* <note>original: *inter duplicem cognitionem & triplicē scholam*</note>, so that through such knowledge one may be led upward according to the three schools / out of time toward eternity / out of the visible toward the invisible / out of the fragile and imperfect toward the unbreakable and perfect: For through knowledge the <term>soul</term> <note>original: *Gemüth*. In this mystical context, it refers to the seat of a person's thoughts, emotions, and spiritual intent.</note> rises above itself to God, from one school into the other. 

The first *knowledge* <note>original: *Cognitio*</note> is natural, through <term>reason</term> <note>original: *Vernunfft*. In this era, "Reason" was often viewed as a lower faculty limited to logic and sensory data.</note> / where one recognizes one thing as true and the other must be a lie / that is / *reason* <note>original: *ratio*</note> cannot reconcile *contradictions* <note>original: *contradictoria*</note>. The second knowledge is called supernatural or divine, *through the mind* <note>original: *per mentem*. Here, "Mind" refers to the higher spiritual intellect that transcends logic.</note> / where one can reconcile two opposing things and recognize them both as true / *for the mind can more easily unite two contradictories* <note>original: *Mens enim facilius potest duo contradictoria unire*</note>, although one could find, write, or teach no divine or mental knowledge / because the *Mind* <note>original: *Mens*</note> is like God / it knows, understands, and sees nothing <note>The author is likely referencing "apophatic theology," the idea that God is so far beyond human understanding that the highest spiritual state is a "holy ignorance" or "knowing nothing" in a rational sense.</note> / and every person to whom God grants His

->## Confession.<-

To correctly understand these three schools and their students, know that there is a twofold knowledge. One is natural, through **reason**, and the other is Divine, Heavenly knowledge through the <term>Mind</term> <note>original Latin: *mentem*. In this context, the Mind refers to the highest spiritual faculty capable of direct, intuitive apprehension of God, distinct from logical reasoning.</note>, in the **Light of Grace**. As far as heaven is from the earth, so far is this heavenly, mental knowledge from earthly, natural knowledge, where <term>reason</term> <note>original Latin: *ratio*</note> rules and where two contradictory things cannot be united, nor can they be recognized as true at the same time—as the **Mind** in the **Light of Grace** can easily do. 

Thus you have two schools: the **Highest** through the **Mind** in the **Light of Grace** in the light of faith <note>original Latin: *in lumine fidei*</note>, and the **Middle School** through **reason** in the **Light of Nature**. In this **second school** <note>original Latin: *Schola secunda*</note>, one studies the Scripture by reasoning and discoursing from one thing by way of another <note>original Latin: *ratiotinando, discurrendo ex uno per aliud*</note>; the rational scholars want to have the "why" <note>original Latin: *quare*</note>—why is it so? For to know is always to understand causes <note>original Latin: *Scire enim est semper causas cognoscere*</note>. 

If a passage or saying <note>original Latin: *dictum*</note> is presented to such a **rational theologian**, he diligently compares the context that comes before and after <note>original Latin: *antecedentia* and *consequentia*</note>; he weighs the passage cited from the text, through all circumstances, until he arrives at some knowledge proven and confirmed by reason <note>original Latin: *ponderiert locum ex textu citatum, per omnes circumstantias, donec perveniat ad aliquam cognitionem ratione probatam & confirmatam*</note>. He himself examines every circumstance of the text from one to the other, until he draws for himself a certain knowledge. For this purpose, he makes great use of the **arts** and **languages**, such as **grammatical speech** in every language in which he theologizes; **Logic** <note>original Latin: *Dialectica*</note> and **Rhetoric** must also help much in this, otherwise he could not be a **Theologian**, a **Bachelor**, or a **Doctor** of the Scripture. # Public Profession of Faith

<meta>catchword: Such</meta>

To bring reason and imagination into the **Mind** <note>original: *Mentem*</note>; [this state] does not discourse, nor does it imagine or fantasize; it understands and knows nothing [in a worldly sense], for it has lost the **distinction of beings, places, and times** <note>original: *discrimen entium, locorum & temporum*</note>. Where nothing is seen or recognized, there is no knowledge, but rather a state beyond all knowledge—unfathomable, incomprehensible, unattainable, and inexpressible. Even though (I say) no mental knowledge could be established there, this must nevertheless happen **to distinguish between nature and grace, and between reason and the mind** <note>original: *ad discrimen naturæ & gratiæ rationis & mentis*</note>.

Because **reason** distinguishes all things, while in the **Mind** all things are found united, it is rightly called Divine and mental. This is because **reason** <note>original: *ratio*</note>, once drawn up into the **Mind** <note>original: *mentem*</note>, no longer operates, and the **Mind** <note>original: *Mens*</note> or God is all in all. Therefore, **mental knowledge** <note>original: *mentalis cognitio*</note> is established and maintained—not because we find a **distinction of things**, or find in God a "this," a "that," a "here," a "there," a "today," or a "tomorrow," etc., but rather so that we may demonstrate the necessity of how a human being must become "mental" <note>meaning spiritualized or operating through the higher Mind</note> by bringing their fragmented, rational knowledge to the **Mind**. In that state, there is only **Divine Folly**, but not yet **Divine Wisdom**. **Divine Folly** can be taught, happen, and be written down, but **Divine Wisdom** cannot.

There are also three schools—that is, there are ways to learn the Holy Scripture:

1. Some students study their **theology** under the tile-roofs of the universities, and this is the **lowest school, a most inept instrument for sacred theology** <note>original: *infima Schola, ineptissimum organum ad sacram Theologiam*</note>.

2. Some study through the help of **reason**, out of the **Light of Nature**, where the **astral spirit** <note>original: *Sterngeist*; in the thought of the era, this refers to the influence of the celestial bodies on the human intellect and physical world</note> is both teacher and student; this is an enmity against God.

3. Some study and learn in the **third school** through grace in the **Holy Spirit** <note>original: *H. Gnist*, a variation of *Geist*</note>, and they attain **perfect knowledge of all things** <note>original: *perfectam omnium rerum cognitionem*</note>.

<summary>The author explains that true spiritual knowledge requires transcending logical reason to reach the "Mind," where all distinctions of time and place vanish. He critiques academic and natural-reason approaches to theology, advocating for a third "school" led by the Holy Spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Mind vs Reason, Divine Wisdom, Divine Folly, Three Schools of Theology, Light of Nature, Astral Spirit, Holy Spirit, mysticism</keywords>

# Confession

To rightly understand such three schools with their students, know that there is a twofold knowledge: a natural one through **Reason**, and the Divine, Heavenly knowledge through the **Mind** <note>original: *mentem*</note>, in the light of Grace. As far as the heavens are from the earth, so far is Heavenly mental knowledge from earthly natural knowledge. In the latter, **Reason** <note>original: *ratio*</note> rules, and two contradictory things cannot be unified, nor can they be recognized as true at the same time—whereas the **Mind** <note>original: *mens*</note> can easily do this in the light of Grace.

Thus you have two schools: the **Highest School** through the **Mind** in the light of Grace, **in the light of faith** <note>original: *in lumine fidei*</note>; and the **Middle School** through **Reason** in the light of Nature. In this **Second School** <note>original: *Schola secunda*</note>, one studies the Scripture **by reasoning and discoursing from one thing to another** <note>original: *ratiocinando, discurrendo ex uno per aliud*</note>. Rational scholars want to have a **"wherefore"** <note>original: *quare*</note>; they ask, why is it so? **For to know is always to recognize causes.** <note>original: *Scire enim est semper causas cognoscere.*</note>

When a **saying** <note>original: *dictum*</note> is presented to a **rational theologian**, he diligently compares the **preceding and following parts** <note>original: *antecedentia* and *consequentia*</note> and weighs the **passage cited from the text through all circumstances** <note>original: *locum ex textu citatum, per omnes circumstantias*</note>, **until he arrives at some knowledge proven and confirmed by reason.** <note>original: *donec perveniat ad aliquam cognitionem ratione probatam & confirmatam*</note> He himself scrutinizes all the circumstances of the text, from one point to another, until he draws out a certain knowledge for himself. To this end, he makes great use of the **arts and languages**, such as **grammatical speech** <note>original: *sermo Grammaticus*</note> in every language in which he theologizes. **Logic** <note>original: *Dialectica*</note> and **Rhetoric** must also assist him greatly; otherwise, he could not be a "theologian," a Bachelor, or a Doctor of the Scripture.

But it is a great defect <note>or "failing"</note> that the Divine Mystery should be subject to human cleverness and reason, through art and languages which...

# Public Confession

<meta>catchword: not</meta>

...come from the stars <note>original: *Gestirn*; likely referring to the influence of the macrocosm or natural world on human intellect</note>; such **Reason** <term>Vernunfft: the natural human capacity for logic, which the author distinguishes from higher spiritual insight</term> is **enmity against GOD and Christ.** <note>Latin: *inimicitia adversus DEUM & Christum*</note> What are the world’s theologians but **rational Doctors, Bachelors, and Masters of the Scripture**? Indeed, they are enemies and they contradict the **pure Divine Truth.** From this, you may perceive the state of theology in these current times. Such a **rational man** <note>original: *rationalis*</note>, however, cannot unify **contradictory statements** <note>original: *contradictoria*</note>; instead, he holds only one thing as **absolutely true** <note>original: *verum absolutè*</note>, while the opposite is a lie—according to the rule: **It is impossible for two contradictories [to both be true]**, etc. <note>Latin: *Impossibile est duas contradictor[ias]*; a fundamental principle of Aristotelian logic</note>. 

For example: "God does not repent" is absolutely true; therefore, "it repented God" <note>referring to biblical passages like Genesis 6:6 where God is said to regret His actions</note> is a **lie** <note>original: *mendacium*</note>. However, because it stands in the Scripture, one must make a **truth** <note>original: *veritatem*</note> out of it **through a condition or perspective** <note>original: *per conditionem, respectum*</note>. Namely: "it repented God" applies to, within, and through the creature who exists in time; but God is unmovable in His eternity, and nothing may repent Him. 

But a **mental man** <term>mentalis: one who operates from the *mens* or higher spiritual mind</term> embraces both **this saying and its opposite** <note>Latin: *hoc dictum & ejus oppositum*</note> and sees both as **most true** <note>Latin: *verissimum*</note> without any qualification, because he must take the **end and the beginning together**; otherwise, he would have only a half, fragmented truth. 

Between these two schools there is yet another; the third may be called the **Sensory School** <note>original: *Sensualis*</note>, which refers generally to the **Academies** <note>original: *Academiæ*</note>, also called High Schools. There, a person hears and learns from another; there, one believes based on **good persuasion**; there, one sees with **other people's eyes**, and wants to know nothing of the **inner eye or hearing**. There, they do not understand the **maxim**: 

¶. **Judgment must be in the judger and not in the object to be judged.** <note>Latin: *judicium oportere esse in judicante & non in judicabili objecto*</note> 
¶ **Understanding must proceed from the understander and not from the thing to be understood.** <note>Latin: *Intellectum proficisci oportere ex intelligente & non ex intelligibili*</note> 

Thus, they also know nothing of the **combination of contradictories** <note>Latin: *combinatione contradictoriorum*</note>. When it comes to the height of their ability, they proceed as was just said of the **second school**: they **compare** <note>original: *conferieren*</note>; but they cannot [unify] two contradictories...

# Public Faith

...come from the stars; such **Reason** <note>original: *Vernunfft*</note> is **enmity against GOD and Christ** <note>original: *inimicitia adversus DEUM & Christum*</note>. What are the theologians of this world other than rational Doctors, Bachelors, and Masters of the Scripture? Indeed, they are enemies and they contradict the pure Divine Truth. By this you may judge the state of theology in current times. Such a **rational man** <term>rationalis; one who relies solely on human logic</term> cannot reconcile contradictory things; instead, he holds only one to be **absolutely true** <note>original: *verum absolutè*</note>, and the opposite is a lie, according to the rule: *It is impossible for two contradictory statements to be true at the same time.* 

For example: "God does not repent" is absolutely true; therefore, "God repented" must be a lie. Yet, because it stands in the Scripture, one must make truth out of it through conditions or perspectives. Namely: "God repents" occurs in, with, and through the creature who exists within time; but God is unmoving in His eternity, and nothing can make Him repent. 

But a **man of the Mind** <term>mentalis; one who perceives through the higher spiritual faculty</term> embraces both this saying and its opposite, and he sees both as most true without any special qualification. This is because he must take the End and the Beginning together; otherwise, he would have only a half, fragmented Truth. 

Beneath these two schools is yet another, which may be called the third, or **Sensual School** <term>Sensualis; the level of outward perception and rote learning</term>. These are commonly the **Academies** <note>The author refers to universities of the time</note>, which are also called High Schools. There, one person hears and learns from another; there, one believes based on good persuasion; there, one sees with other people's eyes and wants to know nothing of the inner eye or inner hearing. In that school, they do not understand the maxim: 

¶ **Judgment must reside in the one who judges and not in the object being judged.** <note>original: *judicium oportere esse in judicante & non in judicabili objecto*</note> 
¶ **Understanding must proceed from the one who understands and not from that which is understood.** <note>original: *Intellectum profiscisci oportere ex intelligente & non ex intelligibili*</note> 

Thus, they also know nothing of the **combination of contradictions**. When they reach their highest potential, they proceed as was just said of the **Second School**: they compare and confer; but two contradictions they can [not reconcile...]

# Confession

The senses rule by drowning out **Reason**; the **Light of Nature** <term>Liecht der Natur: the innate human capacity for understanding the physical world through God-given reason</term> is perverted; all faculties are falsified; **Philosophy is corrupt** <note>Latin: *philosophia corrupta*</note>, its **Physics** <note>original: *Physica*</note> is wrong; the **mathematical arts** <note>Latin: *Artes mathematicæ*</note> and **languages** <note>Latin: *Linguæ*</note> remain separate on their own. The **mentalists** <note>original: *mentalischen*; those operating at the level of the higher Mind or Spirit</note> submerge and drown within themselves the Light of Grace; they can unify all things and penetrate all things with their understanding. In the manner just described, the three schools do not yet stand correctly; but they would be helped, and each would become **perfect and complete** <note>original: *perfect vnnd volkommen*</note>, if the lowest were always swallowed up by its superior—as if the **men of letters** <note>Latin: *literati*</note> in the Academies let their sensual comprehension be swallowed up by...

# Confession

...not unify. The senses rule by drowning out **Reason**; the **Light of Nature** <term>Light of Nature: A concept popular in Renaissance philosophy referring to the innate human capacity for understanding the physical and moral world without divine revelation.</term> is inverted, and all faculties are corrupted. **Philosophy is ruined** <note>Latin: *Philosophia is corrupta*</note>, its **Physics** is incorrect, and the **mathematical arts** and **languages** <note>original: *Artes mathematicæ vnd die Linguæ*</note> remain isolated for themselves. How highly such **Academies** rank may be recognized by any scholar in the **Middle School**, since he can see with his own eyes as well as with the eyes of another. 

But as a blind man stands in relation to one who sees, so the worldly scholars—the **Sensualists** <note>original: *Sensuales*; those who rely solely on physical senses</note> and **Doctors**—stand against the **Light of Nature** in the Middle School. And as one who blinks stands against one who sees, so the **Light of Nature** in the Middle School stands against the **Light of Grace** in the **Heavenly Third School**. **Theophrastus** <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, better known as Paracelsus (1493–1541), a Swiss physician and occultist.</note> mentions such three schools in his **Sagacious Philosophy** <note>Latin: *Philosophia sagax*; Paracelsus's major work on the relationship between man and the cosmos.</note>, which you may read there.
