Those educated in the Middle School, through their **Reason** and the **Light of Nature**, sink and drown the **Mind** <note>Latin: *mentem*; referring to the higher, spiritual intellect above logical reason</note>; therefore, it is also impossible for them to truly unify **two contradictory things** <note>Latin: *duo contradictoria*</note> in reality. To them, only one side is true, while the other is a lie. 

The mental ones <note>Those who operate from the *mens* or spiritual mind</note> sink and drown the **Light of Grace** within themselves; they are able to unify all things and penetrate all things with their understanding. In the manner just described, these three schools do not yet stand rightly; but they would be helped, and each would become **perfect** and complete, if the lowest were always swallowed up by its superior. Natural human knowledge in the **Middle School** <term>Mittelschul: the second level of learning, representing natural reason situated between worldly academics and divine insight</term>, which is driven by the rational Light of Nature, produces sharp wits and wise, learned people, but it does not bring salvation. Divine knowledge in the **light of faith through the mind** <note>Latin: *lumine fidei per mentem*</note> gives all heavenly and earthly wisdom; it brings rest, peace, joy, and eternal life, as in John 17: **"This is eternal life, that they may know You as the true GOD and Jesus Christ, whom You have sent; the knowledge of GOD is the root of eternal life"** <note>original Latin: *Hæc est vita æterna, ut te agnoscant verum DEUM & Jesum Christum, quem misisti, esse, cognitio DEI est ra-dix æternæ vitæ*</note>.

<meta>catchword: is</meta>

The senses do not recognize God; reason is also distant and foreign to Divine knowledge. Only the mind <term>Mens: the higher spiritual intellect, distinct from mere logical reasoning</term> in the light of faith recognizes the Son through the Holy Spirit; and with the Son, we come to the Father. However, because in the union <term>combinatione: the joining or reconciling of opposites</term> the end and the beginning, time and eternity, must come together so that we do not recognize **GOD** only halfway—as the heathens do, who have written much about God according to high intellect, [claiming] that He sees nothing, knows nothing, and hears nothing. For that reason, they have depicted Jove <note>Latin: *Jovem*: the Roman god Jupiter, used here to represent a philosophical, pagan concept of a remote deity</note> with a face without eyes, ears, tongue, or mouth.

While this is certainly correct on its own side—that God remains an Eternal Spirit without passions <term>affectloß: impassible; not subject to human emotions or physical sensations</term>, knowing, hearing, and seeing nothing [in a human way]—it is nevertheless only a halfway knowledge. They have only a half-God, as He is according to His eternity without the creature; thus, they have no God at all, nor Christ. These are the ones in the **Middle School** <term>mittelSchul: the realm of natural reason and worldly philosophy</term> who wish to be themselves without God in the world. 

Those of the senses in the **Lowest School** <term>Schola infima: the level of understanding based purely on physical perception and literalism</term> fall silent over these things when they read the heathen writings. They believe along with the common crowd of the learned that **GOD** knows, sees, and hears all things, as the Scripture also testifies. Although this is equally true, they are nonetheless false and incorrect for the very reason that they do not also recognize and hold the opposite part <note>Latin: *oppositam partem*</note> to be true. 

Therefore, so that we do not persist only in persuasion like cows and calves with the **Cattle-Scholars** <term>ViehSchülern: a derogatory term for those who lack spiritual discernment and follow only their animal instincts or rote learning</term>, and so that we do not remain heathens, but rather draw near to God in Christ Jesus—indeed, remain united with Him—we unite the contradictions <note>Latin: *contradictoria*</note> through the mind <note>Latin: *mentem*</note>. We take the end for the beginning and bring time to eternity; we thus have a whole God who is perfect in all eternity. For instance, "God sees all things" is true according to time [viewed] with eternity; but "God sees nothing"...

<meta>This page continues the author’s exploration of the "paradox of the Trinity." This page addresses the logical contradictions (paradoxes) that arise when trying to describe the Divine using human language.</meta>

# Public Profession of Faith

<meta>catchword: preach/</meta>

[greater] than the other / yet the opposite part <note>original: *opposita pars*</note> is also true: No Person is greater than the other / no Person is before the other. One can also say: The Holy Spirit is a creature / the Holy Spirit is not a creature. The Son is created / the Son is not created / both are true. Now look at the universities <note>original: *hohen Schulen*</note> and certain councils <note>original: *concilia*</note>, how they brand their opponents as heretics and condemn them, and are themselves the arch-heretics / because they recognize only one part as true / and not the other / as the sensory students in the academies <note>original: *Academijs*</note> say: God is not without emotion <note>original: *affectloß*</note> / He grieves as the Scripture <note>original: *Schrifft*</note> testifies. Those in the middle school say: **GOD** is without emotion / He does not grieve: Both are true and both are false: because they only recognize one part <note>original: *unam partem*</note> as true. Those in the third school take both together and do not err / they say: **GOD** does not grieve / and God does grieve / both are most true <note>original: *utrumq; verissimum*</note>. There, no disputing or quarreling is needed / there, no one is condemned / there is golden harmony <note>original: *aurea concordia*</note> in Jesus Christ, who through His cross makes one out of both / and so it should be with all articles of the entire religion / that two contrary things are united and recognized. Therefore, this is also true / God has no beginning / God has a beginning. The Holy Spirit is a creature / the Holy Spirit is not a creature. The lower two schools quarrel earnestly over that which they cannot grasp: they bear witness that they are robbed of faith and of Christ / and they dare to go to war over the Holy Spirit and kill people for the sake of the Gospel <note>original: *Evangelij*</note> / and they would be deserving of fire <note>original: the symbol $\Delta$ refers to fire in alchemical/theosophical shorthand</note> / such that they should be burned / But Christians do not do such things / they do not fight with the sword / nor does Christ teach them to do so. Open your eyes, you theologians <note>original: *Theologi*</note> / look at what you write and teach /

<meta>This page continues the "Public Profession of Faith," moving from the external application of divine law and medicine to the internal state of the "reborn" individual (the Renatus). # Public Profession [of Faith].

Whoever remains in Christ through faith, remains in the Cross and cannot sin. 1. Therefore, he can neither err, fail, nor sin, in that he can unite *two contradictory things* <note>original: *duo contradictoria*</note> and recognize them as true; he can crucify the Scripture and remains crucified to the world with Christ; he remains a child of God born of God, therefore he does not sin; he is not his own, but belongs to Him who has born him.

2. A <term>reborn person</term> <note>original: *Renatus*; in this mystical context, it refers to someone who has undergone a spiritual rebirth or "new birth" in Christ, a central theme in Weigelian and Pietist thought</note> also cannot sin for this reason, and can unite *two contradictory things* <note>original: *duo contradictoria*</note> such that he takes the *Alpha and Omega* <note>original: α & ω, the first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing that God is the beginning and the end</note> together and unites them essentially; he brings the end into the beginning, the earth to heaven, nature to grace, the *creature* <note>original: *Creatur*, referring here to any created thing or being</note> to God. And because he is a creature, he brings himself into God; for he is created through Christ as a new creature for good works and not for evil; therefore he is a child of God born of God, completely retaining the nature and quality of God; hence he cannot sin.

3. Thirdly, a <term>reborn person</term> cannot sin for this reason, and thus brings the beginning into the end, time to eternity, so that neither can be or exist without the other; for how would anything be or remain without its beginning? What would a creature be without its beginning? What would a creature be without its Creator? What would time be without eternity? etc. Man can do nothing without God, and God does nothing without man, whether he be evil or good; but because in the essential union of the end with the beginning the *lawful and indissoluble marriage* <note>original: *legitimum & indissolubile conjugium*; here used as a metaphor for the permanent spiritual union between the soul and God</note> is maintained, a <term>reborn person</term> cannot sin; just as, on the contrary, an unbeliever sins in all things because he lives *outside of lawful marriage* <note>original: *extra conjugium legitimum*</note>.

4. Fourthly, a <term>reborn person</term> cannot sin for this reason, and therefore one cannot be without the other, as daily experience in time and the world testifies, how there must always be two side-by-

The author argues that because this union is a "lawful marriage" between the soul and the Creator, the reborn person is preserved from sin.</summary>

## The 11th Chapter.

### That a natural man errs, fails, and sins in all things / hence no Master, Licentiate, or Doctor of Theology may be a true theologian.

Therefore a <term>natural man</term> <note>original: *Naturalis*; one who relies on human reason alone without spiritual rebirth</note> must err / fail / sin / for he cannot unite <term>two contradictions</term> <note>original: *duo contradictoria*</note> and recognize them as true / that is: He lives according to his natural reason / which strives against the light of grace / in which grace, according to <term>mental knowledge</term> <note>original: *mentalischer Erkäntnüß*; knowledge originating in the "mind" or "apex of the soul" where contradictions are reconciled</note> all things are one.

## Public Confession of Faith

Secondly, the <term>natural man</term> <note>original: *Naturalis*; referring to someone who relies solely on human reason and academic learning without spiritual enlightenment</note> sins in all things because he cannot reconcile <term>contradictions</term> <note>original: *contradictoria*</note> / nor bring the end back to the beginning / through his reason he separates God and <term>creation</term> <note>original: *Creatur*</note> / time and eternity / heaven and earth. Thus he remains distant and a stranger to God / whatever he does / studies and learns / is all out of nature without grace / out of man without God / therefore he can accomplish nothing without sin.

Thirdly, he cannot unite the <term>Alpha and the Omega</term> <note>original: *α & ω*; the beginning and the end</note> together / for he believes that the Beginning and the End, eternity and time, can each exist and persist as <term>perfect</term> <note>original: *perfect.*; complete or whole in itself</note> on its own / which is nevertheless not possible / for just as time cannot exist without eternity / just as little can man exist without God / or the believer without Christ. The natural man wants to be for himself / and God should also be for Himself / he believes he can certainly exist without God / can speak / study / write / pray / etc. without Him. In short / he remains "without God in the world" <note>A reference to Ephesians 2:12</note> / how then can he be a <term>Doctor of Scripture</term> <note>original: *Doctor der Schrifft*; a formal academic title in theology</note> / since he is not <term>spiritually-minded</term> <note>original: *mentalis*; possessing the higher spiritual intellect or 'Mens'</note> / since he does not make God his God / but keeps his own reason / and wants to be his own god / for it is impossible for <term>reason</term> <note>original: *ration*</note> to become the <term>higher mind</term> <note>original: *Mens*; the divine or spiritual faculty of the soul</note> / unless it dies completely.

Reason against the faith does fight /
In the future it will not trust /
When God Himself would comfort bring :
Also / she says : "All is now lost" /
Though the <term>Cross</term> <note>original: *Creutz*</note> has newborn us :

A natural man does not want to die with Christ on the Cross / therefore he also cannot rise with Him to a new life / he remains in the old life in <term>Adam</term> <note>original: *Adam*; representing the fallen, unredeemed state of humanity</note> / a Licentiate / Master / Doctor of Scripture / a deceiver of the land and the people.

Fifthly, the <term>natural man</term> <note>original: *Naturales*; one who relies on human intellect rather than spiritual revelation</note> must sin because he pays no heed to the <term>twofold knowledge</term> <note>original: *duplicē cognitionē*; likely referring to the knowledge of God through both nature and grace</note> and the <term>three schools</term> <note>original: *tres Scholas*; a concept in mystical theology referring to different levels of spiritual education</note>, he remains in his own senses / believes based on good persuasion / what others say / write / oh, inner experience! / he wants to know nothing of the spiritual knowledge in grace <note>original: *mentalischen Erkäntnuß*</note> / he remains in his reason according to nature / he is no heavenly eagle like John <note>Referring to the traditional symbol of St. John the Evangelist, the eagle, representing his high spiritual flight and insight into divine mysteries</note> / he does not want to flutter into heaven / so he says. / Therefore his understanding remains darkened / he perceives little or nothing of divine things / he remains a stranger and far from **GOD** / and is a stranger to the life that comes from **GOD** in Jesus Christ. ## Chapter 12.
### That it is difficult for a natural man to learn Theology and other things.

In this chapter I will write **about the cause of the difficulty of learning Theology and all things** <note>original: *de causa difficultatis discendi Theologiam & omnia*</note>. Why <term>Theology</term>—indeed, the path to Theology—is so long / multifaceted, and difficult / namely, the false Theology of the <term>high schools</term> <note>referring to the academic universities of the time</note>.

<meta>This page continues the discussion from Chapter 12 regarding the difficulty the "natural man" faces in understanding divine things, contrasting the simplicity of true Theology with the burdensome complexity of academic or institutional religious study.</meta>

->**Confession.**<-

But you should know that there is no more than one true **Theology** <note>original: *Theologia*; the study of God and divine things</note> / which is brief / simple <note>original: *schlecht*; in this period, it meant straightforward or unadorned rather than "bad"</note> and entirely easy / which is contained in the writings of the Prophets and Apostles / where **Jesus Christ** is the cornerstone / the beginning / middle and end / **in whom are all things & through whom are all things & for whose sake are all things, outside of whom is nothing** <note>original: *in quo omnia & per quem omnia & propter quem omnia, extra quem nihil*</note>; to Him be praise / honor and glory in eternity / of whom we shall treat in the following **Chapters**.

Now [we shall speak] of the false, mixed **Theology** [created] through human cleverness / and it is the **Theology** of all high schools <note>original: *hohen Schulen*; referring to universities</note> throughout the whole world, of all **synagogues**, of all **consistories** <note>administrative councils of a church</note>, of all **synods—except for the synod of the Apostles in the book of Acts**, and a few others.

Now, why is the **Theology** of all high schools difficult, multifaceted, and tedious?

Firstly because / they require effort / labor / worry / and great diligence / whoever intends to become a **Licentiate, Doctor, or Master of Theology** or a preacher requires much effort to learn such things / he must move and travel here and there among the **Academies** / no one will say that it happens in 2, 3, or 4 years / etc. He must confess that it requires much staying awake / studying / memorizing / worry and anxiety / and that this is so is proven by the second reason.

Secondly / that one must first learn many arts / such as **Grammar, Dialectic, and Rhetoric** <note>the "Trivium" of classical education</note> / for there, no **Peter** from the fishing net, nor **Andrew**, nor **John** is accepted / rather, they must be good **Logicians** / who can **dispute for and against** <note>original: *pro & contra*</note> and brand their opponent a heretic / who have **acquired eloquence** / who have a good **Latin style** / everything must proceed out of and according to art [or skill] / as **Erasmus** wrote in his book ***On the Art of Preaching*** <note>original: *Erasmus de arte concionandi*; referring to the work *Ecclesiastes* by Desiderius Erasmus</note>.

--- Page 67 ---
<meta>This page continues the critique of academic or "institutional" theology. Having established that the "natural man" finds divine things difficult, the author now lists the specific intellectual and linguistic burdens placed upon students by universities, contrasting these human requirements with the true guidance of the Holy Spirit.</meta>

->**Public Faith**<-

...whether or not it costs much effort / labor / worry / and time to study Grammar / Dialectic / Rhetoric / etc. / those who wish to learn it must themselves confess. One does not select fools for this / but rather capable people / for no one is permitted to become a Licentiate / Doctor / or Bachelor of Theology / unless he is a good **logician** <note>original: *Logicus*</note> / that is / unless he has thoroughly studied the **arts of learning** <note>original: *artes discendi*</note> alongside the **Languages** <note>original: *Linguis*</note>.

Thirdly / that one must not only bring the arts to this task—the **trivium** and **quadrivium** <note>The "trivium" (grammar, logic, rhetoric) and "quadrivium" (arithmetic, geometry, music, astronomy) made up the seven liberal arts of medieval and Renaissance education.</note> / the seven liberal arts / but one must also have **knowledge of the languages** <note>original: *Linguarum cognitionem*</note> / Latin / Greek / Hebrew / Syrian / Chaldean / etc. Therefore it is said: "That is a good <term>Theologian</term> <note>original: *Theologus*</note> / a true Doctor of Theology / for he is a good Artist and Linguist." But how much labor and time does it cost / before one learns Hebrew / Greek / and Latin? Truly / one must shed much sweat / much staying awake / and much money belongs to it / without which such things cannot be studied. They regard art and languages as so necessary to Theology / that they say: It is impossible to be a true theologian without such means. Indeed / art and languages are the **organs** <note>original: *Organa*</note> or tools through which one can rightly learn / read / teach / preach / and **rightly divide the word** <note>original: *ὀρϑοτομεῖν* (orthotomein) / *rectè secare verbum*; a reference to 2 Timothy 2:15, implying that only those with linguistic training can interpret Scripture correctly.</note>.

Thus they make a human art out of Divine Theology / they wish to cast the Holy Spirit under the stars / under the animal-spirit / the dizzy-spirit / the **star-spirit** <note>original: *Stern-Geist*; in the mystical tradition of writers like Jacob Boehme, this refers to natural, earthly intelligence governed by the celestial bodies, which they believed was inferior to divine revelation.</note> / for only the star-spirit becomes a Licentiate / Master / or Doctor of the Scripture / a Rabbi. But the Holy Spirit does not know such Rabbis / nor has He been with them / as their lives and writings sufficiently testify. No one is ordained / or called to the <term>Ministry</term> <note>original: *Ministerio*; the office of a pastor or priest</note> unless he has—

# Confession.

...for they have learned **human** or **false theology** <note>original: *Theologiam humanam oder Falsam*</note> in the synagogues. No one in these lands is ordained unless he has studied at universities / unless he be of the **star-spirit** <term>The "star-spirit" (German: *Stern Geist*) refers to a person whose knowledge comes from the natural, worldly intellect or "astral" influences, rather than direct divine inspiration.</term> / a scholar / a Doctor / Master / or Bachelor of Scripture. I say that they would truly not have let me come to the **preaching office** <note>original: *Predigamt*</note> in this place / nor permitted me to deliver any sermon / if I had not been a Master / and known the **seven liberal arts** <note>The traditional curriculum of grammar, logic, rhetoric, arithmetic, geometry, music, and astronomy.</note> and languages. I was myself of the opinion that no one was fit to serve the church **without languages and arts** <note>original: *sine Linguis & artibus*</note>.

But when I came upon the little book called the **German Theology** <note>original: *Deutsche Theologia*; a mystical treatise from the 14th century highly influential on Martin Luther and later radicals.</note> and Thomas à Kempis’s **On the Imitation of Christ** <note>original: *de Imitatione Christi*</note>, as well as **Tauler** <note>Johannes Tauler (c. 1300–1361), a famous German Dominican mystic.</note>, etc., there I found the rogue / the liar in myself / seeing that with my preaching I was as useful to the church as the Devil is to the Word of God. The **Holy Bible** <note>original: *H. Biblia*</note> does not allow itself to be regulated by the stars / that is / by the arts and languages or by human cleverness. From the stars come the Doctors / Masters / and Bachelors of arts and faculties; but the Holy Spirit is above the whole **firmament** / he is the **third heaven** <note>original: *tertium Coelum*; a reference to the vision of St. Paul in 2 Corinthians 12:2.</note>. It is from there that one should become a Doctor / taught by God (as in the Gospel of John). This is what the universities call **enthusiasm** <term>The term "enthusiasm" (German: *Enthusiasterey*) was used mockingly by established churches to describe "fanatics" who claimed to receive direct, private revelations from God.</term> / they want to save their learning for the **eternal academy** <note>original: *æternam Academiam*</note>.

<margin>The inner word-book and light of witness.</margin>

The fourth cause is that the church also requires an **abundance of books** <note>original: *copiam librorum*</note>; she is not content with the Holy Bible, but must have many **Fathers** <note>The "Church Fathers," early influential Christian theologians.</note> in three languages and many commentators. But God has given us one book / the Holy Bible / the Old and New Testament / and those who cannot read have that very same thing within themselves: Jesus Christ / God and man. For everything that is contained in the whole of Scripture / is contained in this one word: **Love God and your neighbor as yourself.** Also: **Whatever you want others to do to you / you should also do to them** / that is...

# Public [Confession of] Faith

# Confession [of Faith]

...believe just to please others; for who is he that could believe this writing of mine, unless he be taught by God?

The 5th reason is that one learns it [theology] not in Heaven from God, but in Hell from men; not in the Light which is Christ, but in the Darkness that is Adam; not from the Spirit, but from the Letter and the Flesh, etc. The Apostles say: Our way of life is in Heaven <note>A reference to Philippians 3:20.</note>—that is, in the Spirit, in the Light, in Christ, and not on Earth—which means: If we wish to learn <term>theology</term> <note>original: *Theologiam*</note>, then we must not seek it among men in the flesh, on Earth, or in Hell, but with God in Heaven, who is a Light and is Theology itself, through His Son Jesus Christ, whom all <term>high schools</term> <note>A common term for universities in the early modern period.</note> reject and do not wish to know.

Now, to prove that they study their theology not in Heaven, but in Hell with great care, toil, and anxiety, hear this:

They are all full of pride and not humble in Jesus Christ; therefore they strive after honors, seeking <term>degrees</term> <note>original: *gradus*</note> and titles for their names, etc. They learn their languages and arts in pure pride from men at the unchaste, impure places of the high schools, where one finds more harlots than angels; is that not Hell and Darkness? They drink, gamble, womanize, play instruments, and go <term>roaming the streets</term> <note>original: *gassatum*; a Latin-derived term for rowdy nightly carousing or wandering by students.</note>. I myself have seen more pride, unchastity, and harlots in the high schools over thirteen years than humility, virtue, purity, chastity, angels, etc. If they could understand me, I would prove to them that their teaching, learning, and <term>graduating</term> <note>original: *promovieren*; refers to the process of obtaining higher academic degrees.</note> were nothing but Hell. Three things create Hell and bring eternal darkness, unrest, and damnation.

# Confession.

### Chapter 13.
## That true Theology is short, simple, and easy, and can be learned without effort or labor.

> He who learns one thing learns all things:
> He who learns many things learns nothing.
<note>original: "Qui unum discit omnia discit: / Qui multa discit nihil discit."</note>

He who learns, studies, and seeks one thing, learns all things easily and maintains eternal peace. He who learns, studies, and seeks many things learns nothing and finds no peace either here or there; this is a true rule which shall serve us in this chapter to prove how the right and true <term>theology</term> <note>The study of God or divine things; the author argues here that it is an internal experience rather than an academic degree.</note> is so entirely simple, small, and short that it is unbelievable.

For firstly, it is easy, simple, small, humble, and so quickly learned because it requires absolutely no exertion of the mind or body, no effort or labor; only praying, seeking, and knocking—not a human or Pharisaic <note>Referring to the Pharisees described in the New Testament; here meaning a performative, legalistic, or superficial way of praying.</note> prayer (for in that there is toil, displeasure, and vexation), but praying, seeking, and knocking according to the teaching of Christ, <margin>Matthew 6, 7.</margin> Such praying and seeking requires no will or desire, nor any thinking or speculating; only to trust oneself to faith without all searching, only to turn inward into oneself <term>unto heavenly silence</term> <note>original: *ad silentium coeleste*; a state of meditative quietude where the soul stops its own activity to hear God.</note>, waiting for GOD in the quiet <term>Sabbath</term> <note>original: *Sabbath*; used here not as a day of the week, but as a spiritual state of rest and cessation from human works.</note>, which is the praying, seeking, and knocking. He who prays thus seeks the Kingdom of GOD, and persists, and finds it within himself; he needs to run nowhere to study such things. In prayer, no specific number of years is set; God gives it

Secondly, <term>theology</term> is easy to learn because one does not need to learn the <term>arts of speaking</term> <note>original: *artes dicendi*; these were the traditional "Trivium" of the liberal arts: Grammar, Dialectic (logic), and Rhetoric.</note> such as Grammar, Dialectic, and Rhetoric for it, nor use any human eloquence. # Confession

and he learns or recognizes. The false must be united with their arts and languages, which are fragile and fall away from a person, abandoning them at death. But the righteous are also united with that which they learn and recognize; they recognize Jesus Christ, who is the entirety of <term>Theology</term> <note>The author defines theology here as the person of Christ himself, rather than a system of academic rules.</note>. They become essentially one with Him, so that He dwells in them and they in Him, as in John 14:17. This union is one and eternal; therefore they remain one in Christ eternally, and He does not abandon them in death. *The one who knows becomes one with the thing known, the one who knows with that which is known, and the one who understands with that which is understandable* <note>original: *Cognoscens fit unum cum cognito, sciens cum scito, intelligens cum intelligibili*</note>. 

However, the false **theologians** do not want to be essentially united by God; they do not want Christ and His life to be physically present within them. They are certainly able to speak and preach about Him, even though they remain outside of Him; that is their anxiety, toil, and distress—their darkness and damnation. But the righteous learn their **Theology** in such a way that they are essentially united with it and remain so, so that they can see and taste Christ according to His indwelling, His working, and His power. *By seeing they know; by knowing, they understand; by understanding, they touch; by touching, they penetrate; and by penetrating, they are united with the object of understanding. Once united with Christ, they can do all things and learn all things without labor* <note>original: *Videndo cognoscunt cognoscendo, intelligunt intelligendo, tangunt tangendo, penetrant penetrando uniuntur cum intelligibili. Uniti autē cum Christo possunt omnia, discunt omnia sine labore.*</note>. Therefore, true **Theology** is quite simple and easy; it requires no tedious time, toil, or work—only a good, pure heart that loves God.

Fourthly, **Theology** is easy and brief because one does not need a multitude of books. Only one book is necessary, and that is the **Holy Bible**, for the person who can read. But for the person who cannot read, the **Holy Bible** is given just as well, for he hears it read and preached, and he has the book within himself; that is the Anointing <note>A reference to the "unction" or "anointing" mentioned in 1 John 2:27, which the author interprets as the internal presence of the Holy Spirit or Christ.</note>, that is, Jesus Christ, the Word <note>original: *h. e. Jesus Christus / verbum*</note>.

# Confession

## The 14th Chapter.

### That Scripture must contain contradictions / otherwise it would not be Scripture / and one would have no perfect knowledge <note>original: "perfectam cognitionem"</note>.

Just as this world could not be or remain a world without opposites <note>original: "contraria"</note>, it must have opposites within itself for its own permanence; these opposites sustain this world, and the four pillars of the world are themselves opposites. For fire <note>original: "ignis"</note> is against <term>▽</term> <note>Alchemical symbol for water</note>, cold is against heat / etc. At length, although the Elements as spirits are <term>without qualities</term> <note>original Greek: ἄποια (apoia), meaning "without properties"</note>, that is, they are without specific quality in their balanced state <note>original: "temperatür"</note>, yet the things that grow and are produced from them are cold / warm / etc. In the same way, Scripture must also remain through opposites / otherwise it would not be Scripture. In all articles of faith and religion, contradictions must be maintained / otherwise one could not obtain a true and complete foundation. The following reasons testify that this is so / though I do not recount them all / for another person might be able to set down many more.

I. First, Scripture consists of opposites because it presents nothing other than Jesus Christ / who is both GOD and Man / one whole, perfect Person composed of Spirit and flesh / of eternity and time.

II. Secondly, because God and the Word sink themselves into the Person and reveal themselves in JESUS Christ / who is God and man / God is the Word and the Word is God / both are one in the Heavenly being / neither exists without the other. Now the Spirit or the Word has become flesh / for God is a Spirit / and the Spirit or God is the Word, therefore—

## From whence has the Scripture flowed?

Fifthly, one must therefore take end and beginning together, eternity and time, so that the nature and quality of eternity in heaven, and the type and quality of time on earth, are observed and maintained. For it is the property of eternity <note>original Latin: *æternitatis proprium est*</note> not to be affected, not to know [suffering], to hear without being disturbed, and not to die; but it is the property of time <note>original Latin: *temporis proprium est*</note> to be moved, to be affected, to be disturbed, and so forth. And one cannot exist without the other, and one must remain within the other, like the night in the day, light in the darkness, and conversely <note>original Latin: *è contra*</note>, night in day and darkness in light; the outer man in the inner, the inner in the outer, and this produces opposites.

<meta>This page continues the "Response" regarding the essential unity of the soul with God, transitioning from the idea that we learn only what is already within us to the mystical process of how "Imagination" and "Knowledge" transform a mortal person into a spiritual being united with the divine.</meta>

->Public Profession of Faith<-

Knowledge. **Imagination** <note>original: *Imaginatio*</note> is mortal; yet it can make itself immortal. **When this is done** <note>original: *quo facto*</note>, the whole person becomes **Spiritual** <note>original: *Mentalis*</note>; that is, they possess **a standing and not a falling soul** <note>original: *Animam stantem, & non cadentem*</note>, and can do all things, whatever they only think. **Christ** says: **"This is eternal life, that they may know You as the true God, and Jesus, whom You have sent, to be the Christ," John 17.** Likewise: **The knowledge of God is eternal life** <note>original: *Cognitio Dei est vita æterna*</note>. Whoever now recognizes or studies God, so to speak, understands God and knows what God is. **He who understands God, who is incomprehensible and intangible, penetrates Him who is impenetrable, and is penetrated by Him. He who penetrates God is united with Him essentially; this happens through ignorance and heavenly silence, by which we are joined to Him who escapes all knowledge and understanding.** <note>The author is describing "apophatic" theology—the idea that God is best known by moving beyond words and intellectual concepts into a "heavenly silence."</note> Thus, a person can unite bodily with God through **understanding and knowing** <note>original: *intelligere, cognoscere*</note> and can become that which they know—namely, God or the Son of God. Thus, a person becomes exactly that which they study, learn, and recognize; indeed, they find themselves and first truly come to themselves, for they are all things and have all things within themselves. Here we should see that Christ did not institute His Holy Supper to wipe away sin through it, but rather in His memory, so that He may be our <term>clay: *Limus*, referring to the "dust of the ground" from which man was formed; here suggesting Christ is the very substance of the new spiritual body</term> and food; for we are from Him, therefore we must be preserved in Him. Earthly nature shows us that we eat ourselves <note>Likely referring to the metabolic process where the body consumes nutrients to build itself</note>, and must become that which we eat. So also in the Light of Grace, the **new creature** must eat itself—that is, to be and become that which we eat. For this remains true once and for all: John 6. We must eat Christ’s flesh and drink His blood, for He is our life, our **clay** <note>original: *limus*</note>, out of which we are born, in which we grow through true knowledge. In faith, we eat Christ’s flesh and drink His blood; so we must become exactly that which we eat—that is, we become Christians, a Christian through Christ, and must be essentially one with Him, not [merely] by **imputation** <note>original: *imputativisch*; a reference to the theological debate over whether a person is merely "counted" as righteous by God (imputed) or actually becomes righteous in their essence (essential/infused).</note> from

This is the truth: that we must be essentially united in Christ and remain so, like the branch on the vine. They will be cast out and thrown into the eternal darkness with their **Imputed righteousness** <note>original: *Justitia imputativâ*; the mainstream Protestant view that God "credits" righteousness to a sinner's account rather than actually changing their nature</note>. Just as the Father dwells bodily in the Son, so Christ dwells bodily in the **reborn person** <note>original: *Renato*</note>. Since we are all now fed and watered with the Holy Spirit through and with Christ’s flesh and blood, the gracious and kind Lord has established a memorial of His wonders. He takes bread and wine and gives us therein His body and blood to eat and drink, so that we should reflect upon such great wonders and never forget them. Such a memorial admonishes us that we should recognize, learn, and study exactly that from which we are made. We are from Christ; we are from His flesh and bone, and He is not from us. Therefore we must become that which we eat, and it must be revealed within us that we are of Divine birth; for we are from God; we have the Spirit from God.

Yet we are also like God and like Christ. If only we study, learn, and recognize nothing other than ourselves, then we have recognized all things. ## Wherein primarily true Theology resides,

It is impossible to learn the true <term>Theology</term> <note>original: *Theologiam*; the study of the nature of God and religious truth</note>, that is, the Holy Scripture, perfectly and without heresy without the <term>contradictions</term> <note>original: *contradictoria*; logical statements that seem to deny one another</note>; one must necessarily unify the contradictions and recognize them both as true, for the Scripture is always contrary and set against itself in its verses. If you do not look at the <term>opposites</term> <note>original: *contraria*; points of opposition</note>, you cannot protect yourself from error and heresy; you must dispute and quarrel against others.

Secondly, it is therefore also impossible to unify the <term>opposites</term> <note>original: *contraria*</note> for the sake of Scripture and to recognize both as true without the Alpha and Omega <note>the first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, representing Christ as the beginning and the end</note>, without the Beginning—that is, without the true Eternal God, who died on the Cross and was buried, and rose from the dead on the third day, who says: "I was dead and behold, I live for eternity. I am the Alpha and Omega, the Beginning and the End, the First and the Last." The worldly scholars believe they know the Alpha and Omega very well, but their speech, writings, books, and lives prove it quite otherwise.

## Chapter 17.

### On Contradictions and What Alpha and Omega Are.

That there must be <term>contradictions</term> <note>original: *contraria*. In this context, it refers to seemingly opposing statements in the Bible that must be reconciled through spiritual insight.</note> in the Holy Scripture—several reasons for this were recounted above, especially in Chapter 14; take these to heart. But if you wish to see from a distance, as it were, why there must be contradictions in the Scripture, and that one cannot study the Scripture without the essential unification of these <term>opposites</term> <note>original: *contrariorum*</note>, then look at where the Scripture came from, what it presents to us, and for what purpose it was given. 

Thus you will find nothing else within it but your eternal God, who says: I am the <term>Alpha and Omega</term> <note>original: *$\alpha$ & $\omega$*, the first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing the totality of God.</note>, the Beginning and the End, the First and the Last—that is the Eternal True God. He is not the Spirit alone, for He is too bright for you; nor is He the Word or the letter alone, for it is too dark for you. Rather, He is the unique Person, the **Morning Star**, into whom the Spirit and the Word have entered and taken on human nature, and who is called **J. C. G. M.** <note>likely an abbreviation for *Jesus Christus Gott Mensch* (Jesus Christ, God-Man).</note>. 

This **Morning Star** stands between Day and Night, says **Paulus Lautensack** <note>A 16th-century organist and painter from Nuremberg known for his complex, visionary diagrams of the Tabernacle and divine mysteries.</note>, just as the natural course of the heavens testifies. The **Morning Star** is between the Sun and Moon, which rule over Day and Night. The Sun is too clear for us, and the Moon is too dark for us. So this Person is the Beginning according to the Spirit, and the End according to the Word. He is the First according to the eternal God, and is the Last according to the temporal Word; He is everything. In this, the **Holy Trinity** <note>original: *H. Trinitet*</note> is revealed to us: heavenly and earthly, eternal and temporal...

...[and cannot] make all contradictions <note>original: *contradictoria*</note> one in Christ Jesus; they want to make themselves learned.

The 3rd school unifies two contradictions because they are completely one in the mind <note>original: *in mente*</note>, in faith, and in Christ. It takes the letter and the spirit together, brings the end into the beginning, and stands in the Alpha and the Omega <note>The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing the totality of God as the beginning and the end.</note>. It speaks with truth: God is grieved; God allows Himself to repent; God has passions <note>original: *affect*. In this context, it refers to God having "affections" or emotional responses to humanity, a point of theological debate.</note>. Understand this: the end with the beginning, time with eternity, according to the three earthly and heavenly persons. For if God had no passions, no will, no action, no repentance, then we would have no God at all, no Christ, or we would have only half a God who would be of no use to us—just as the false theologians <note>original: *Pseudotheologi*</note> do not rightly recognize the Trinity, as witnessed by the **Augsburg Confession** <note>original: *Augustana Confessione*. The primary confession of faith of the Lutheran Church, written in 1530.</note> and other writings. Although they think they know the Alpha and Omega very well and have a completely pure, perfect philosophy <note>original: *Philosophia*</note>, these five points and the Last Judgment will make them blush with shame and sufficiently convince them that they never rightly recognized the Trinity.

Here on earth they are arrogant; they dare to label other righteous teachers as heretics, drive them away, or even kill them for the sake of a single priestly article <note>original: *Pfaffenartickels*. A derogatory term for dogmas or rules created by the professional clergy to maintain power.</note> that they only half know and understand—the other half is a lie to them. They forbid printing so that no books except their own come into print, by which one can sufficiently recognize what they know of the Alpha and Omega.

The 1st school maintains *opinions* <note>original: *δόξας* (doxas), meaning "notions" or "appearances" rather than certain truth</note>, that is, opinions <note>original: *opiniones*</note>; they may be called the *opinion-mongers*.

The 2nd school maintains *paradoxes* <note>original: *Paradoxa*</note>; they take the Alpha alone or the Omega alone; they are not complete and have no complete understanding.

The 3rd school maintains *orthodoxy* <note>original: *ὀρθοδόξα* (orthodoxa), meaning "right teaching" or "correct glory"</note>; it takes the Alpha and Omega together essentially; it recognizes the Trinity from the inside and outside. Among their schools there is no war, bickering, nor disputation, nor writing of false books. For as soon as someone wanted to dispute about the articles [of faith], he would give witness against himself that his faith was without love and stood only in [human] reason...

## ->Public Profession of Faith<-

I say: as long as we remain together in this manner under

<header># ## ### ->Profession [of Faith]<-</header>

...makes all contradictions <note>original: *contradictoria*</note> one in Christ Jesus; they [the other schools] want to make themselves learned.

The 3rd school unifies two contradictions for this reason: that they are entirely one in the mind <note>original: *in mente*</note>, in faith, and in Christ. It takes the letter and the spirit together; it brings the end into the beginning; it stands in the Alpha and Omega <note>The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing the totality of God as the beginning and the end.</note>. It speaks with truth: "God is grieved," "God allows Himself to repent," "God has passions" <note>original: *affect*. In this theological context, the author is arguing against "impassibility"—the idea that God cannot feel or change—suggesting instead that God is personally and emotionally involved with humanity.</note>. Understand this: [reconcile] the end with the beginning, time with eternity, and according to the three earthly and heavenly Persons. For if God had no passions, no will, no agency, and no repentance, then we would have no God at all, no Christ, or we would have only a half-God who would be of no use to us—much like the false theologians <note>original: *Pseudotheologi*</note> who do not rightly recognize the Trinity, as witnessed by the Augsburg Confession <note>original: *Augustana Confessione*. The primary confession of faith of the Lutheran Church, established in 1530.</note> and other writings. Although they think they know the Alpha and Omega very well and possess a completely pure philosophy <note>original: *Philosophia*</note>, these five points and the Last Judgment will make them blush with shame and sufficiently prove that they never truly recognized the Trinity. Here on earth they are bold; they dare to brand other righteous teachers as heretics, to drive them away, or even to kill them for the sake of a single priestly article <note>original: *Pfaffenartickels*. A dismissive term for narrow, clerical dogmas or rules created by the professional clergy.</note> which they only half-know and understand. The other half is a lie to them. They forbid printing so that no books but their own come into press; by this, one can sufficiently recognize what they know of the Alpha and the Omega. The 1st school brings forth opinions <note>original: *δόξας* (doxas) or *opiniones*</note>; those who follow it may be called "opinionists" <note>original: *opinistæ*</note>. The 2nd school brings forth Paradoxes <note>original: *Paradoxa*</note>; they take the Alpha alone or the Omega alone. They are not whole and have no complete understanding. The 3rd school brings forth true teachings <note>original: *ὀρθόδοξα* (orthodoxa)</note>; it takes the Alpha and Omega together in essence. It recognizes the Trinity from within and without. Among its schools, there is no war, bickering, disputation <note>original: *disputation*</note>, nor the writing of false books. For as soon as someone wanted to dispute the articles, he would bear witness against himself that his faith was without love and stood only in [human] reason <note>original: *Vernunfft*</note>...

# ->Profession of Faith<-

...make all contradictions <note>original: *contradictoria*</note> one in **Christ Jesus**; they wish to make themselves learned.

The 3rd school unites two contradictions <note>original: *duo contradictoria*</note> because they are entirely one in the mind <note>original: *mente*</note>, in faith, and in **Christ**. It takes the letter and the Spirit together, brings the end into the beginning, stands in the **Alpha** and **Omega** <note>The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing the totality of God as the beginning and the end.</note>, and speaks with truth: "God is grieved," "God allows Himself to repent," "God has emotion <note>original: *affect*</note>." Understand this by seeing the end with the beginning, time with eternity, and the three earthly and heavenly Persons <note>Referring to the Trinity.</note>. For if God had no emotion, no will, no action, no repentance, then we would have no God at all, no **Christ**, or we would have only half a God who would be of no use to us—just as the false theologians <note>original: *Pseudotheologi*</note> do not rightly recognize the **Trinity**, as witnessed by the *Augsburg Confession* <note>original: *Augustana Confessione*; the primary confession of faith of the Lutheran Church (1530).</note> and other writings.

Although they think they know the **Alpha** and **Omega** quite well and possess a completely pure and perfect philosophy <note>original: *Philosophia*</note>, these five points and the Final Judgment will make them blush with shame and sufficiently convince them that they never truly recognized the **Trinity**. Here on earth they are bold; they dare to brand other righteous teachers as heretics, drive them away, or even kill them for the sake of a single priestly article <note>original: *Pfaffenartickel*; a derogatory term for rigid dogmatic rules established by the institutional clergy.</note> which they only half-know and recognize; the other half is a lie among them. They forbid printing so that no books but their own are published, by which one can sufficiently recognize how little they know of the **Alpha** and **Omega**.

The 1st school brings forth opinions <note>original Greek: *doxas*; Latin: *opiniones*</note>; they may be called "opinionists" <note>original: *opinistæ*</note>. The 2nd school brings forth paradoxes <note>original: *Paradoxa*</note>; they take the **Alpha** alone or the **Omega** alone; they are not whole and have no complete understanding. The 3rd school brings forth "beyond-opinions" <note>original Greek: *hyper-doxa*, meaning truths that exceed human opinion or glory.</note>; it takes **Alpha** and **Omega** together in essence <note>original: *wesentlich*</note>. It recognizes the **Trinity** from within and without. Among these schools, there is no war, bickering, nor disputation, nor the writing of false books. For as soon as someone wished to dispute about these articles, he would bear witness against himself that his faith was without love and stood only in [human] reason...

# Chapter 18.

## That this number of the Beast, 666, encloses and encompasses all things—indeed, that it is the essence and life of all things.

Whoever can essentially unify two contradictory things and recognize them as true takes together the Alpha <note>original: α</note> and Omega <note>original: ω</note>; that is, he is unified with Him who is Alpha and Omega, the Beginning and the End, the First and the Last. All true knowledge in the Divine Light is neither shadow nor accident <note>original: *Accidens*. In traditional philosophy, an "accident" is a non-essential quality of a thing. The author argues that true spiritual knowledge is "essential"—it changes the very nature of the person.</note>, but it is being—an essential union of the knower with the known. Just as one who knows theology <note>original: *Theologiam*</note> becomes a theologian, not an "imputed" <note>An "imputed" status is one assigned legally or externally (like a title); an "essential" status is one that is lived and true to the person's soul.</note> one, but an essential theologian. Whoever knows **God** becomes **God**—a child of **God**. Whoever knows **Christ** becomes a **Christian**. Whoever knows astronomy <note>original: *Astronomiam*</note> becomes an astronomer, and so it is with all other arts and faculties, and manual crafts, and so forth. The false philosophers <note>original: *Philosophi*</note> and theologians learn their subject only halfway. But the righteous bring the end into the beginning, the outer into the inner, the human to **Christ**, the creature <term>Creatur: everything created by God, particularly humanity</term> to **God**, and time to eternity. Thus, Adam is transplanted into **Christ**; thus, nature is drawn into grace; the members are unified with the head <note>original: *Häupt*</note>. To speak scripturally: all the books of the Old and New Testaments are unified in the Revelation <note>original: *Apocalypsi*. The final book of the Bible, also known as the Apocalypse.</note>, like limbs joined to the head. For just as **Christ** is the

Such a conclusion and comprehension are shown by the *visions* <note>original: *visiones*</note> of the prophets, who saw the four beasts and the four wheels, in Ezekiel chapters 1 and 10. The first beast was like a lion, the second like a human, the third like a calf, and the fourth like an eagle. In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and God was the Word; the same was in the beginning with God; all things were made through the same, and without the same nothing was made that was made, John 1. As Moses also testifies: In the beginning God created heaven and earth. If one says that the Law and the Gospel are one, a single teaching, he is satisfied. If another says Law and Gospel are not one but are distinct, he also remains satisfied. Likewise, if one writes: Adam is the Tree of Life and of Death, he agrees; and if another says on the contrary: Christ is the Tree of Life and of Death, it counts the same for him. In short, a perfect theologian <term>Theologus</term> <note>A scholar of divine things; in this context, a mystical seeker</note> quarrels with no one, for he is at peace with himself; but a false or half-theologian opposes others, for he is at odds with himself. The reason why the true theologian needs to bicker, war, or dispute with no one is that he takes the Alpha <note>original Greek: "α"</note> and Omega <note>original Greek: "ω"</note> together; and while in his reason <term>vernunfft</term> <note>The faculty of logical, earthly understanding</note> he can rightly distinguish, in his higher mind <term>mente</term> <note>Latin: *mens*; the spiritual or intuitive intellect</note> he can rightly unify. No one is good except God alone, and everything He creates, works, or makes is good; that is essence and cannot be nothing. Now, if a person does not turn toward the right or the left, but remains like an innocent child / like a walking stick / an ax / a hammer / etc., then God is good in him, with him, and through him. But if he turns to the right or to the left—that is, from God toward himself—he then experiences good and evil within himself, and takes it upon himself as if it were his own; for the essence that he is and has received from God is good and belongs to God, but he, for himself, becomes weak and like nothing. Let no man belong to another who can belong to himself:* <note>original Latin: "Unitas Unum, quod est a se, in se, de se, per se, nullius egens. From which he was taken and created: as from the **mud of the earth** <note>Latin: *limo terrae*</note> he is of Nature and has the choice; he may let himself be governed by the **mud of the earth**, or he may rule and dominate over the **mud of the earth**. He is from God and can rule over God; he can resist Him or can be governed and mastered by GOD. One also sees both cases: the Apostles, Prophets, and all the **Reborn** <note>Latin: *Renati*; those who have undergone a spiritual transformation</note> let God's Spirit or Christ govern, lead, guide, and master them; because of this, they are blessed here and in the hereafter. But the unbelievers, along with the world, resist the Holy Spirit, dominate GOD, and strike Christ to the ground, which no one can deny. It is indeed quite terrible that we should rule over or be above Him who gives us breath, spirit, light, life, power, and movement; we take our essence from Him...

...seduced—that is, she becomes a serpent, so that she can and is able to do all things. From this you shall see that I.C. G.M. <note>These initials likely refer to a specific person or a radical theological concept, possibly "Iesu Christi Gnaden-Mittel" (Means of Grace of Jesus Christ) or a polemical target, identified here as the Antichrist/Beast.</note> is the beast with seven heads and ten horns, in, with, and through the creature. He is the dragon according to the spirit, yet not without the temporal creature; he is the false prophet and the **Whore of Babylon** according to the word, yet not without the temporal creature. He is, and is not, and yet he is. Now follows the true understanding of the **Whore of Babylon**.

## Chapter 20.

If you understand the Holy Trinity in a twofold, threefold, and ninefold way—the forbidden tree and the tree of life, the two serpents (heavenly and earthly)—then you have the mystery of the **Whore of Babylon**. There is nothing more to be written or spoken concerning the **Whore of Babylon**. A word to the wise is sufficient <note>original: "Sapienti sat dictum"</note>.

->E N D.<-

<summary>This page concludes a mystical interpretation of the Fall, defining the "imagination" as the feminine principle that leads to self-will, and identifying institutional religious figures with the Whore of Babylon.</summary>
<keywords>Imagination, Eve and Adam, The Fall, Whore of Babylon, Beast of Revelation, Holy Trinity, Mysticism</keywords>

## D I A L O G U E
## on Christianity:
### That is:

# A Christian,

->highly important and necessary col-<-
->loquy or conversation between three most eminent<-
->persons in the world: the Listener,<-
->the Preacher, and<-
->Death.<-
<note>The original Latin titles for these characters are Auditor (the Listener), Concionator (the Preacher), and Mors (Death). This "dialogue" format was a common teaching tool in the 17th century.</note>

->How man, taught by God,<-
->reborn from God, united with Christ<-
->bodily, internally, and externally,<-
->becomes blessed and righteous, and not<-
->apart from Him.<-
<note>Weigel emphasizes an "internal" Christianity, suggesting that true faith is an inward transformation rather than just external ritual.</note>

->Composed by<-

### Master Valentin Weigel, former
### pastor at Zschopau.
<note>Valentin Weigel (1533–1588) was a famous German mystic. His works were considered controversial and were mostly published posthumously by his followers to avoid censorship during his lifetime.</note>

->Printed at Neustadt by<-
->Johann Knuber, in the year of Christ<-
->1618.<-

<summary>This is the title page for Valentin Weigel's "Dialogue on Christianity," a 1618 publication that explores the internal spiritual rebirth of a believer through a conversation between a Listener, a Preacher, and Death.</summary>
<keywords>Valentin Weigel, Dialogue on Christianity, mysticism, spiritual rebirth, 17th-century theology, Zschopau, Johann Knuber</keywords>
